| This box: [view](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Myanmar_civil_war_infobox)[talk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template_talk\:Myanmar_civil_war_infobox)[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Template\:Myanmar_civil_war_infobox) |  |

The **Myanmar civil war**[^cite_note-60] began following the [military coup](66567997.md) on 1 February 2021. The seizure of power triggered mass [anti-coup demonstrations](66612294.md) and a violent crackdown by the [Tatmadaw](20394.md) (Myanmar armed forces), which significantly escalated [the country's longstanding insurgencies](13299430.md).[^cite_note-61][^cite_note-62]&#x20;

In response to the coup, the [exiled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government-in-exile) [National Unity Government](67413816.md) (NUG) and major [ethnic armed organisations](48437766.md) repudiated the [2008 Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Constitution_of_Myanmar) and called instead for a democratic [federal state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federalism_in_Myanmar).[^cite_note-63] Besides engaging this alliance, the ruling military junta the [State Administration Council](66589972.md) (SAC), led by [Min Aung Hlaing](38278640.md), contended with other anti-regime forces in areas under its control.[^cite_note-64] The insurgents are apportioned into hundreds of armed groups scattered across the country.[^cite_note-NYT-Ragtag-65]&#x20;

As of March 2023 the [United Nations](31769.md) estimated that since the coup, 17.6 million people in [Myanmar](19457.md) required [humanitarian assistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanitarian_assistance), 1.6 million were internally displaced, and over 55,000 civilian buildings had been destroyed.[^cite_note-NN-66] In May 2026, the [United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees](31964.md) (UNHCR) reported that around 299,000 people had fled to neighboring countries such as [Bangladesh](3454.md), [India](14533.md), and [Thailand](30128.md).[^cite_note-67]&#x20;

By 2022, the opposition controlled substantial, though sparsely populated, territory.[^cite_note-68] By October 2023, the [Tatmadaw](20394.md) controlled under 40% of the country, though it maintained that it controlled around two-thirds of the country's 330 townships.[^cite_note-69][^cite_note-noendinsight-70][^cite_note-71] In the second half of 2023, [Chinland Defense Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinland_Defense_Force) in [Chin State](493366.md) had captured a majority of the state, with a few holdouts in urban areas and along the [India–Myanmar border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India%E2%80%93Myanmar_border) remaining. In October 2023, the Tatmadaw began facing manpower issues, with desertions and low morale being extremely common. This coincided with a [major offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1027) by the [People's Defense Force](67819275.md) (PDF) and [Three Brotherhood Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Brotherhood_Alliance) in the west of the country, which took 80 bases, 220 SAC positions, and several towns by 28 November 2023.[^cite_note-morale-72]&#x20;

October 2023 saw the beginning of a series of concurrent anti-SAC offensives. In [Operation 1027](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1027), anti-SAC forces seized [Laukkai](24155033.md), the capital of [Kokang Self-Administered Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kokang_Self-Administered_Zone).[^cite_note-laukkai-73] [Operation 1111](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1111) besieged the state capital of [Loikaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loikaw).[^cite_note-irrloikaw-74] [Arakan Army](38106209.md) (AA) seized [Mrauk U](2636746.md), among other towns, in an [offensive in northern Rakhine state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rakhine_offensive_\(2023-present\)).[^cite_note-mrauku-75] By February 2024, thousands of the SAC's soldiers had surrendered without a fight, including six Tatmadaw generals.[^cite_note-BBC-losing-24-1-2024-76] The [Sit-tat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sit-tat) uses [terror tactics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tactics_of_terrorism) against the population, including [burnings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_by_burning), [beheadings](70802.md), [mutilations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutilation), [war rape](17572536.md), [torching villages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scorched_earth), and a massive [aerial bombing](323167.md) campaign that has displaced nearly three million people.[^cite_note-77] The [Myanmar Air Force](9484818.md) has dropped more bombs per capita than have been dropped in the [Russo-Ukrainian war](70149799.md).[^cite_note-NYT-Ragtag-65] A group of observers wrote that the Sit-tat's forces remain formidable, with external allies and economic resources.[^cite_note-78]&#x20;

In March 2024, anti-SAC forces in southeastern Myanmar captured [Demoso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demoso) and [Papun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papun),[^cite_note-demoso-79][^cite_note-hpa-80] bringing the number of [district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Myanmar)-level towns captured by anti-SAC forces to eight. In June, [Matupi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matupi,_Myanmar) was captured by Chin resistance.[^cite_note-Matupi-81] The [Three Brotherhood Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Brotherhood_Alliance) then restarted Operation 1027, capturing [Kyaukme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukme,_Shan_State) in June[^cite_note-Kyaukme-82] and two more district-level towns in July.[^cite_note-Mongmit-83] On 3 August, the MNDAA [captured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Lashio) [Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashio), the largest town in northern Shan State and headquarters of the Tatmadaw's [Northeastern Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeastern_Regional_Military_Command_\(Myanmar\)).[^cite_note-Lashio-84] On 20 December 2024, the [Arakan Army](38106209.md) [captured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Ann) the Tatmadaw's [Western Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Regional_Military_Command_\(Myanmar\)) headquarters in [Ann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ann,_Myanmar), Rakhine State.[^cite_note-85]&#x20;

After the deadly [2025 Myanmar earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Myanmar_earthquake), the NUG declared a two-week pause on offensives,[^cite_note-quakecf-86] and the SAC announced a [ceasefire](592591.md) from 2 April to 22 April,[^cite_note-87] despite continuing airstrikes on rebel groups.[^cite_note-20_day_ceasefire-88] On 31 July, the SAC announced its dissolution and transferred power to the [National Defence and Security Council](47091432.md) under the chairmanship of Min Aung Hlaing.[^cite_note-89][^cite_note-90] Continued junta counteroffensives saw success in northern Shan state, retaking the district capital of [Kyaukme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukme) on 2 October 2025.[^cite_note-kyaukmeretaken-91] The TNLA withdrew from [Mogok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mogok) and [Mongmit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongmit) due to supply issues and the MNDAA fully withdrew from [Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashio) under Chinese pressure.[^cite_note-tnlawithdraw-92][^cite_note-lashiowithdraw-93] Fighting has continued variously in all regions and states of Myanmar, including [Naypyidaw Union Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naypyidaw_Union_Territory).&#x20;

## Background

### Internal conflict in Myanmar

Main article: [Myanmar conflict](13299430.md)

See also: [List of ethnic armed organisations in Myanmar](48437766.md)

Map of insurgent activity in Burma in 1948

Map of insurgent activity in Burma in 1953

Map of insurgent activity in Burma in 1948 (top) and 1953 (bottom)

Insurgencies have been ongoing in Myanmar since 1948, largely along ethnic lines. [Communists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_\(Burma\)) and the [Karen National Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_National_Union) opposed the central government.[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95] During the 20th century, several prominent [ethnic armed organizations](48437766.md) (EAOs) rose and fell in influence and control. Larger rebel factions such as the [Kachin Independence Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kachin_Independence_Army) (KIA) formed in response to the [1962 coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1962_Burmese_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat).[^cite_note-Pavković,_2011:_476-96] The [8888 Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8888_Uprising) also drove protestors to ethnic rebel territory, forming [Bamar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bamar_people) militias.[^cite_note-97]&#x20;

The [Tatmadaw](20394.md) took control of the government directly and severely weakened ethnic insurgent groups, destroying most of their bases and strongholds through the 1990s,[^cite_note-98] including [Kokang](1635498.md) and [Karen State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_State).[^cite_note-CNN-2t-99][^cite_note-Ray_Pagnucco_and_Jennifer_Peters-100]&#x20;

As part of reforms, the [2008 Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Constitution_of_Myanmar) created [self-administered zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-administered_zone) with increased autonomy, without embracing a [federal system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_system). The 2015 [Nationwide Ceasefire Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationwide_Ceasefire_Agreement) (NCA) was signed between eight EAOs and the central government,[^cite_note-rfaNCA-101] but began to fall apart by 2018, due to alleged violations by Tatmadaw soldiers.[^cite_note-102] Many non-signatories continued the conflict. In late 2016, four non-signatories of the NCA, including the KIA and [Arakan Army](38106209.md), formed the [Northern Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Alliance_\(Burma\)) and resumed war.[^cite_note-Nadi-103]&#x20;

### 2021 coup d'état and protests

Further information: [2021 Myanmar coup d'état](66567997.md) and [Myanmar protests (2021–present)](66612294.md)



Thousands of protesters participating in an anti-[junta](66589972.md) rally in [Yangon](57404.md), February 2021

On 1 February 2021, the [Tatmadaw](20394.md) overthrew the elected government in [a coup d'état](66567997.md), forming a [State Administration Council](66589972.md) (SAC) and detaining [National League for Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_League_for_Democracy) (NLD) leadership. [Min Aung Hlaing](38278640.md) was installed as [the *de facto* ruler](56636725.md) of the country.[^cite_note-104]&#x20;

The Tatmadaw claimed that the [2020 general elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Myanmar_general_election) had 8.6 million voter irregularities, but presented no evidence. The coup has been a way to reestablish the military's absolute dominance, after a decade of civilian rule.[^cite_note-105]&#x20;

## Prelude

Main article: [2021 Myanmar protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Myanmar_protests)

The bloody repression of [anti-coup demonstrations](66612294.md) led to the creation of armed groups to fight the SAC. These groups then aligned with the [National Unity Government](67413816.md) (NUG), formed by ousted parliamentarians, under the name of the [People's Defence Force](67819275.md) (PDF). The PDF and the NUG declared a "defensive war" against SAC rule in September 2021.[^cite_note-106]&#x20;

### Armed protesters

By late March 2021, dozens of protesters per day had been travelling to Myanmar's border areas to enlist in and train under one of the country's many insurgent groups,[^cite_note-fishbeinetal2021-107] elevating the risk of a countrywide civil war.[^cite_note-108] The [Committee Representing Pyidaungsu Hluttaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_Representing_Pyidaungsu_Hluttaw) (CRPH) also proposed the formation of a "Federal Armed Force" to combat the military,[^cite_note-MMN30321-109] and in late March the [Arakan Army](38106209.md) (AA) threatened to end its ceasefire with the military should the latter "persist in massacring civilians".[^cite_note-brotherhoodthreat2021-110]&#x20;

In late March, protesters increasingly began arming themselves with homemade weapons in an attempt to defend themselves against attacks by the military. Clashes with soldiers and [IED](265112.md) attacks against administrative buildings and police stations became more common and protesters slowly became armed resistance.[^cite_note-111]&#x20;

After about 30 years of dormancy, the [People's Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Liberation_Army_\(Myanmar\)) (PLA), the armed wing of the [Communist Party of Burma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_Burma) (CPB), became active again on 15 March 2021 when communist fighters crossed from China into [Kachin State](493369.md), where the Kachin Independence Army gave them weapons.[^cite_note-112] By August 2021, the CPB had established a new armed wing to fight the SAC.[^cite_note-113] Over the next two years, the PLA presence grew in [Tanintharyi Region](15162240.md), where they fought alongside the PDF, claiming to have 1,000 active troops in December 2023.[^cite_note-114]&#x20;

### Renewed ethnic conflict

The unrest across the nation and increased need for SAC troops in previously peaceful urban areas strengthened EAOs. The Kachin Independence Army had already been on the offensive since February and [seized the military's base of Alaw Bum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Alaw_Bum) near the town of [Laiza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laiza) on 25 March 2021.[^cite_note-KIAbizasusual2021-115] The next day, the [Karen National Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_National_Liberation_Army) (KNLA) attacked a military base, killing 10 SAC soldiers and taking others hostage in their first attack since the protests began.[^cite_note-116] The next day saw the [2021 Kalay clashes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Kalay_clashes), where protesters first openly used homemade weapons against soldiers, targeting security forces attacking a protest camp.[^cite_note-117]&#x20;

SAC declared that it would cease all military operations on 29 March 2021 and hold bilateral negotiations with ethnic armed groups. But the Kachin Independence Army continued its offensives, saying the Myanmar Army had not in fact ceased operations.[^cite_note-KIAbizasusual2021-115] Seven insurgent groups who were signatories to the [Nationwide Ceasefire Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationwide_Ceasefire_Agreement) aligned themselves with the [National Unity Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Unity_Government) (NUG), including the [All Burma Student Democratic Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_Burma_Student_Democratic_Front) (ABSDF) and the [Karen National Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_National_Union) (KNU).[^cite_note-118] The [Northern Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Alliance_\(Myanmar\)), comprising the [Arakan Army](38106209.md), the [Ta'ang National Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ta%27ang_National_Liberation_Army), and the [Myanmar National Democratic Alliance Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myanmar_National_Democratic_Alliance_Army), attacked a police station in [Naungmon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naungmon), Shan State, killing at least 10 police officers and indicating their disregard of the SAC's call for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-119] In response, on 11 April 2021, the military junta launched [a counterattack to recapture the Alaw Bum base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Alaw_Bum) using airstrikes and ground troops, but had to retreat amidst heavy casualties.[^cite_note-120]&#x20;

On 26 April 2021, the [Battle of Mindat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Mindat) became one of the first large-scale conflicts arising from the 2021 coup. The [Chinland Defense Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinland_Defense_Force) (CDF) began armed resistance in [Mindat, Chin State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mindat,_Chin_State) and the SAC declared [martial law](50477.md).[^cite_note-121] After a soldier allegedly fired at protesters, fighting between the two sides erupted.[^cite_note-122] The battle lasted four days, killing 30 SAC soldiers and leaving Mindat abandoned as more than 10,000 people fled the area.[^cite_note-123]&#x20;

## Timeline

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Myanmar civil war (2021–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Myanmar_civil_war_\(2021%E2%80%93present\)).

### Onset of formal resistance and war (May 2021 – August 2021)

On 16 April 2021, pro-democracy politician [Min Ko Naing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Min_Ko_Naing) announced the formation of the [National Unity Government](67413816.md), with members of ethnic minority groups in senior roles. As part of the announcement he said that ousted leaders [Aung San Suu Kyi](2847.md) and [Win Myint](37228839.md) would retain their positions and asked the international community to recognize their government over the SAC.[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125] Throughout April, informal clashes with protesters intensified, such as in [Taze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taze,_Myanmar) when protesters fought back against soldiers with hunting rifles and firebombs on 8 April.[^cite_note-126]&#x20;

The National Unity Government declared the formation of an armed wing, the [People's Defence Force](67819275.md) (PDF), on 5 May 2021, a date often cited as the start of the civil war. The PDF was formed to protect its supporters from SAC attacks and as a first step towards a Federal Union Army.[^cite_note-127] The PDF clashed with the Tatmadaw in the town of [Muse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muse,_Myanmar) on 23 May, killing at least 13 members of Myanmar's security forces.[^cite_note-128]&#x20;

In early June, fighting erupted in [Myawaddy District](15144411.md) where the military and Karen [Border Guard Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Border_Guard_Forces) battled against a combined Karen and PDF force, leaving dozens of SAC troops killed.[^cite_note-Irr6321-129] Members of the Karenni PDF in [Kayah State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayah_State) also captured and destroyed several Tatmadaw outposts near the state capital, [Loikaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loikaw).[^cite_note-130] Towards the end of May, the Tatmadaw used artillery and helicopters to strike PDF positions in Loikaw and [Demoso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demoso).[^cite_note-131] On 30 May, the KIA joined the PDF in a battle against SAC troops in [Katha Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katha_Township), killing eight SAC soldiers. Fighting also sprouted up in other Kachin State townships, including Putao, Hpakant and Momauk.[^cite_note-132]&#x20;

While there were fewer conflict deaths between May and September, there were still many armed clashes and a spike in early June.[^cite_note-bbc1-133] Two dozen local officials appointed by the military were assassinated throughout the month of June with hundreds of bombings at police stations, banks and government offices.[^cite_note-etwp-134] On 22 June, SAC forces using armoured vehicles raided a safehouse of the PDF in Mandalay, detaining several fighters.[^cite_note-135] Myanmar security forces killed at least 25 people in another raid in [Tabayin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabayin).[^cite_note-136] These attacks occurred in Central Myanmar, also known as Anya, an area that had rarely seen armed violence in recent times.[^cite_note-iissdry-137] On 2 July, troops assaulted several villages in [Sagaing Region](493361.md) and reportedly killed 41 civilians. *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* described how Myanmar was sliding toward "bloody anarchy".[^cite_note-etwp-134]&#x20;

#### Declaration of war

On 7 September 2021, the NUG declared a [state of emergency](298608.md) across the nation and launched what they called the "people's defensive war" against the SAC.[^cite_note-138] The declaration of war increased the number of skirmishes and clashes between PDF militias, EAOs and the SAC across the country.[^cite_note-iiss-139] According to the NUG in September 2021, over 1,700 SAC soldiers had been killed and 630 wounded in fighting during the preceding three months.[^cite_note-140] Several major clashes took place from September to October in [Chin State](493366.md), [Sagaing Region](493361.md), [Magwe Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magwe_Region), [Kayah State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayah_State) and [Shan State](493376.md).[^cite_note-141][^cite_note-142]&#x20;

On 18 September, the [Pa-O National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pa-O_National_Army), a pro-SAC militia active in the [Pa-O Self-Administered Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pa-O_Self-Administered_Zone), aided the SAC in capturing a resistance base near [Aungban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aungban).[^cite_note-143]&#x20;

By late September 2021, 8,000 residents of [Thantlang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thantlang) town, Chin state, fled to [Mizoram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mizoram), India after their houses were set ablaze by the SAC army.[^cite_note-144] Ambushes by anti-SAC forces in [Shan State](493376.md) also killed at least 20 soldiers.[^cite_note-145] On 16 November 2021, SAC forces overran and captured the base camp of Kalay PDF in southwestern [Sagaing Region](493361.md), killing 2 fighters and capturing 9 Kalay PDF [medics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medics).[^cite_note-146]&#x20;

In October, SAC-controlled media reported that at least 406 junta informants had been killed and 285 wounded since 1 February in targeted attacks by resistance forces.[^cite_note-147]&#x20;

### Initial conflict (September 2021 – August 2022)

#### 2021–2022 dry season campaigns

According to analyst Matthew Arnold, the civil war's momentum had passed a critical threshold by the end of the 2022 dry season, when revolutionary sentiment had grown into a broader social and armed resistance that the SAC could no longer suppress.[^cite_note-Arnold-148] Towards the end of 2021, direct armed clashes gave way to more bombings, [explosive devices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Explosive_devices) and landmines. The PDF, with strong ground support from local communities, attacked soft government targets like [police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myanmar_Police_Force) stations, outposts and military-owned businesses. Through these, the resistance became more organised as they seized weapons, underwent training and communicated between units through the help of the NUG and allied EAOs.[^cite_note-bbc1-133] According to the [Karen National Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_National_Union), roughly 2,200 SAC soldiers and militiamen were killed in the first half of 2022.[^cite_note-149]&#x20;

##### *South-eastern Myanmar*



[Moe Bye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongpai) Reservoir

On 17 November 2021, dozens of SAC soldiers ambushed an outpost of the [Moebye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongpai) PDF in [Pekon Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pekon_Township), Shan State, forcing the outnumbered PDF soldiers to retreat.[^cite_note-150] At least four SAC soldiers were killed during a four-day clash in [Hpruso Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hpruso_Township) with the KNDF and [Karenni Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karenni_Army).[^cite_note-151]&#x20;

On 14 December, around 200 [Tatmadaw](20394.md) troops searched the [Karen National Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_National_Union) (KNU)-controlled town of [Lay Kay Kaw Myothit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lay_Kay_Kaw_Myothit) near the [Thai border](65361745.md), arresting people suspected to be activists or members of the PDF.[^cite_note-152] On 20 December, SAC forces burned down nineteen houses in Kunnar, [Loikaw Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loikaw_Township) after taking it from the KNDF the week before.[^cite_note-153]&#x20;

On 24 December, more than [35 people were massacred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mo_So_massacre) when they were ambushed by SAC troops outside the village of [Mo So](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mo_So) in [Kayah State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayah_State).[^cite_note-154] Two staff members of the aid group [Save the Children](436885.md) were among those killed.[^cite_note-155] The [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) condemned the attack and called for a "thorough and transparent investigation" into the incident.[^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157]&#x20;

Throughout February and March 2022, the SAC carried out repeated air strikes against civilian targets in villages in [Shan](493376.md), [Kayin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayin_State) and [Kayah States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayah_State). On 17 February alone, around 20 SAC soldiers and 20 resistance fighters were killed in clashes in [Mobye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongpai), southern [Shan State](493376.md).[^cite_note-158] Witnesses described soldiers systematically looting jewelry, cash, vehicles, and other valuables. [Amnesty International](18947898.md) reported these actions as [collective punishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_Punishment) against the country's ethnic minorities.[^cite_note-159]&#x20;

Fighting broke out in parts of [Loikaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loikaw) on 14 April.[^cite_note-160] The number of refugees on the Thai border increased after increased combat in [Kayin State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayin_State).[^cite_note-161] On 15 April, SAC soldiers suffered at least 30 casualties after being pushed back by the KNLA at the battle for [Lay Kay Kaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lay_Kay_Kaw).[^cite_note-162]&#x20;

##### *Central Myanmar*



[People's Defence Force](67819275.md) fighting in [Sagaing Region](493361.md), 2022

The [Dry Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dry_Zone_\(Myanmar\)), the historical heartland of Myanmar, had rarely seen armed violence in the [modern conflict in Myanmar since 1948](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_conflict_in_Myanmar) as a predominantly Buddhist and [Bamar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bamar) farming region. The fighting in the [Anya theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anyar_theater) of Central Myanmar starting in 2021 changed this trajectory. Without the presence of EAOs, the Bamar PDF groups are characterized as local cells acting autonomously towards simple goals and directed towards the 2021 coup. In the 2021–2022 dry season, the PDFs began to work more closely together and coordinate towards larger goals.[^cite_note-iissdry-137] In early 2022, resistance forces were fighting in [Monywa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monywa), the capital of [Sagaing Region](493361.md).[^cite_note-163] Resistance attacks on the SAC saw the SAC retaliate on civilians[^cite_note-164] Targeted personnel attacks increased, killing various SAC personnel and destroying equipment.[^cite_note-165] The PDF also suffered losses, with 12 fighters killed in a battle in [Khin-U Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khin-U_Township).[^cite_note-166] Many cities saw violent clashes during 2022's [Union Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Day_\(Myanmar\)).[^cite_note-167] [Mandalay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandalay) also saw fighting, with casualties on both sides.[^cite_note-168]&#x20;

##### *Northern Myanmar*

Throughout the 2021–2022 dry season, various groups in [Northern Myanmar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Myanmar) carried out ambushes against military outposts and convoys. The [Kachin Independence Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kachin_Independence_Army) (KIA) and the PDF attacked convoys in [Mohnyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohnyin_Township)[^cite_note-169] and [Hpakant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hpakant_Township) townships.[^cite_note-170] In October, they also partially shut down gold mining operations run by SAC allies.[^cite_note-171] After an ambush near [Shwegu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shwegu), the Tatmadaw responded with airstrikes and ground attacks against KIA bases in [Hpakant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hpakant) and [Mohnyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohnyin_Township) Townships.[^cite_note-172] In early February, the KIA assaulted several military bases in Kachin and Shan States, reportedly burning one in Hpakant Township down. The SAC responded by increasing airstrikes and sending reinforcements to the area.[^cite_note-173]&#x20;

The [Chinland Defense Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinland_Defense_Force) (CDF) and the [Chin National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_National_Army) (CNA) raided and ambushed outposts and convoys in [Matupi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matupi_Township)[^cite_note-174] and [Mindat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mindat_Township) Townships.[^cite_note-175] In December, the Tatmadaw recaptured the town of [Thantlang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thantlang) from the CDF in an offensive that destroyed over a quarter of the town's buildings.[^cite_note-176]&#x20;

On 14 January 2022, units of the CNA moved into Senam village, south of [Tamu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamu_\(Myanmar\)), in neighboring [Sagaing Region](493361.md) to attack a base run by the Indian-based [People's Liberation Army of Manipur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Liberation_Army_of_Manipur). After several hours of fighting, between 10 and 20 Manipuri rebels and 1 CNA fighter were killed.[^cite_note-177]&#x20;

##### Yangon and other regions

During this time, there were several cases of [guerilla warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guerilla_warfare) across Myanmar using homemade explosives, including several accidents killing resistance fighters. On 17 June 2021, an army vehicle exploded in [Tamwe Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamwe_Township), Yangon, allegedly killing several military personnel.[^cite_note-178] On 14 December, Tatmadaw troops captured 12 suspected resistance fighters after several bombs accidentally exploded in [Hlaing Thar Yar Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hlaing_Thar_Yar_Township), Yangon.[^cite_note-179] [Urban warfare](319027.md) became less practical, so resistance forces began targeting SAC-aligned officials. According to SAC-aligned sources, 367 SAC-appointed officials were assassinated in targeted attacks between February 2021 and February 2022.[^cite_note-180] Resistance forces also began targeting the homes of SAC pilots in [Yangon](57404.md) in response to airstrikes on civilians.[^cite_note-181]&#x20;

Fighting also occurred in other [Bamar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bamar)-majority regions. On 31 January 2022, at least 36 SAC soldiers were reportedly killed in ambushes over three days in Magwe, Sagaing and [Tanintharyi regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanintharyi_region) and Chin, Shan and Kayah states.[^cite_note-182]&#x20;

#### 2022 monsoon decrease in intensity



[Karenni Nationalities Defence Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karenni_Nationalities_Defence_Force) soldiers, September 2022

The intensity of fighting decreased during the [monsoon](57630.md) season.[^cite_note-iiss-139] Resistance forces were advantaged by the rainfall as the SAC could not carry out air strikes as easily.[^cite_note-183] In June, resistance groups achieved control of 40–50% of the country. [Arakan Army](38106209.md) claimed to administer most of [Rakhine State](493373.md) with an independent government. [Chin National Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_National_Front) and [CDF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinland_Defense_Force) made plans to establish a new government. The KIA and the [Wa State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wa_State), a neutral de facto independent region of Myanmar, consolidated expanded territories.[^cite_note-184] However, the [Myanmar Army](9503548.md) retained tight control of almost every city in Myanmar and most of the country's natural resources, including important jade mines.[^cite_note-185] During this time, the PDF were also unable to move beyond rural guerilla tactics. [Duwa Lashi La](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duwa_Lashi_La), acting president of the NUG, cited the lack of weaponry and international support as reasons for the prolonged conflict.[^cite_note-tharoor-186]&#x20;

On 31 May 2022, a [bombing](47628.md) killed one person and injured nine others near the [Sule Pagoda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sule_Pagoda) in [Yangon](57404.md), the largest city in Myanmar. State media accused the [People's Defence Force](67819275.md) of being responsible for the attack, which the PDF denied.[^cite_note-187] A July clash in [Pekon Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pekon_Township), Shan State also killed around 40 SAC soldiers and 11 PDF fighters.[^cite_note-188]&#x20;

##### *Massacres and executions*

The military deployed its time-tested counterinsurgency methods in what has been called a "hammer approach" of bombing and burning villages and massacring civilians to flush out rebel groups. However, the approach was ineffective because they were unable to consolidate power or deter resistance.[^cite_note-tharoor-186]&#x20;

Myanmar military forces executed at least 37 villagers in the [Mon Taing Pin massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mon_Taing_Pin_massacre) in May 2022 after shelling the village of [Mondaingbin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mondaingbin), Sagaing Region with heavy artillery.[^cite_note-189] The SAC forces entered the local [Buddhist monastery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaung), conscripted young male villagers briefly before executing them and other captives by a [stupa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stupa).[^cite_note-190] Following a raid in Se Zin village, Kachin state, SAC soldiers set fire to more than 400 homes, detaining some 400 people in August 2022. [Radio Free Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Free_Asia) reported 100 of the prisoners were killed either through an [extrajudicial massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extrajudicial_killing)) by security forces or due to horrific prison conditions.[^cite_note-191]&#x20;

On 23 July 2022, the [State Administration Council](66589972.md) announced that it had executed four political prisoners, including [Zayar Thaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zayar_Thaw) and [Kyaw Min Yu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaw_Min_Yu), which was the first use of [capital punishment in Myanmar](71368642.md) since the late 1980s.[^cite_note-192] The men had been accused of helping the resistance movement.[^cite_note-cruel-193] The event was widely seen as a provocation to escalate the ongoing conflict by the Tatmadaw.[^cite_note-194] The international community, including United Nations Secretary-General, the G7 nations and the European Union strongly condemned the executions.[^cite_note-195][^cite_note-cruel-193]&#x20;

On 16 September 2022, the Burmese military killed 11 children and wounded another 17 in the [Let Yet Kone massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Let_Yet_Kone_massacre), as part of an airborne strike conducted against a school Sagaing Region.[^cite_note-196] The military claimed that the village was harbouring resistance fighters from the KIA and PDF.[^cite_note-197] The attack was widely condemned by the [international community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_community), including the United Nations and European Union.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199]&#x20;

Later in September 2022, retired Brigadier General [Ohn Thwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohn_Thwin), mentor to [State Administration Council](66589972.md) vice-chairman Senior General [Soe Win](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soe_Win_\(general\)), was assassinated by anti-SAC guerrilla groups in [Yangon](57404.md). This assassination caused an increase in security on high-ranking SAC personnel.[^cite_note-200]&#x20;

#### Breakdown of Arakan ceasefire, monsoon 2022

In early 2022, the [Arakan Army](38106209.md) and the junta clashed again in northern [Rakhine State](493373.md). On 8 February, Arakan Army and junta forces clashed on at least two occasions in [Maungdaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maungdaw) in Rakhine State. Fighting broke out on 4 February when junta troops carried out a sneak attack on an AA outpost near the Letpan Mountains northeast of Mee Taik Village, killing an AA [sentry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Picket_\(military\)), according to AA spokesman Khaing Thukha. Three hours of clashes were also reported on 6 February. The clashes raised fears of a breakdown of the informal ceasefire between the AA and the military which had been in place since November 2020.[^cite_note-201] Further clashes in northern Maungdaw on the night of 7 February killed two civilians.[^cite_note-202] Several junta troops, including a major, were also killed in the attack.[^cite_note-203]&#x20;



The Bangladesh-Myanmar border

Between June and August 2022, the informal ceasefire reached in late 2020 between the [Arakan Army](38106209.md) (AA) and the junta broke down. The AA had consolidated control during this period, avoided the initial violence of the war, and introduced many public services and local administrators in northern [Rakhine state](493373.md). With the military's attention diverted to increasing resistance elsewhere and increasing popular support for an alliance with the NUG, the AA sought to expand its influence into southern Rakhine.[^cite_note-204] Rhetoric from AA leader [Twan Mrat Naing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twan_Mrat_Naing) in June grew more provocative with military spokespeople stating that the AA was inviting conflict.[^cite_note-205] Armed clashes resumed in July after the junta launched an airstrike against an AA base in [Kayin State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayin_State), killing 6 AA soldiers. AA retaliated in [Maungdaw Township](15160625.md) and western [Chin State](493366.md) in late July and early August. By late August, land travel to northern Rakhine required passing a series of checkpoints and all public transport ships had ceased operation due to river and land blockades.[^cite_note-206]&#x20;

On 16 August 2022, two mortar shells fired by the [Myanmar Army](9503548.md) landed in a [Rohingya](1918632.md) refugee camp in [Bangladesh](3454.md), killing one man and injuring five others. [Myanmar Army](9503548.md) helicopters allegedly entered Bangladeshi air space to attack the [Arakan Army](38106209.md) and fired a shell within Bangladeshi air space. Two days later, [Bangladesh](3454.md) summoned Myanmar ambassador Aung Kyaw Moe to strongly protest the land and airspace violations.[^cite_note-207][^cite_note-Daily_Star-208] In October 2022, Bangladeshi Foreign Minister [AK Abdul Momen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AK_Abdul_Momen) made a statement that border bombings by Myanmar had stopped after he met with the [Chinese ambassador to Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh%E2%80%93China_relations), Li Jiming.[^cite_note-209]&#x20;

### Escalation of the civil war (September 2022 – November 2022)

#### Increased resistance efforts

In mid-October 2022, [NUG](67413816.md) issued a statement calling for the victory of the [Spring Revolution](66612294.md) by the end of 2023. This call to action was followed by increased fighting by the resistance forces in urban areas and in Southeastern Myanmar.[^cite_note-at20221104-210] This development took place in the wake of the junta torching at least 20 villages in the [Sagaing](493361.md) and [Magway Regions](493360.md) as part of a "[four cuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_cuts)" strategy of attacking civilians to weaken anti-regime movements. According to Sagaing-based resistance spokespeople, many victims of arson then joined the resistance.[^cite_note-211] The urgency of the resistance was likely prompted by the looming elections planned by the [State Administration Council](66589972.md).[^cite_note-at20221104-210] The fragmented nature of the grassroots elements of the PDF became more organized in 2022 through the command of the NUG and from cooperation with various EAOs- especially the [Kachin Independence Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kachin_Independence_Army) (KIA).[^cite_note-noendinsight-70]&#x20;

The [Karenni Nationalities Defense Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karenni_Nationalities_Defense_Force) (KNDF) claimed in January 2023 that 1,692 regime troops and 211 resistance fighters were killed since the 2021 coup, 293 civilians had been killed by the regime, and 126 displaced civilians died while fleeing their homes in Kayah and Shan states in 2022.[^cite_note-212]&#x20;

##### *Highland attacks*

The [Karen National Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_National_Liberation_Army) stepped up fighting, besieging the major town of [Kawkareik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawkareik) on the Thai border in 21 October 2022 [Battle of Kawkareik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Kawkareik).[^cite_note-at20221104-210] The battle began with surprise attacks on the [Asia Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Highway_Network) and at government offices within the town. Resistance forces looked poised to take the town, but ultimately withdrew two days later after facing junta air strikes and strategically drawing junta troops away from nearby positions.[^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214] Four days later, undeterred KNLA-led forces seized a junta Light Infantry Battalion base in [Kyain Seikgyi Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyain_Seikgyi_Township).[^cite_note-215]&#x20;

In [Shan State](493376.md), clashes between PDF forces near [Inle Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inle_Lake) and the [Pa-O National Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pa-O_National_Organisation) (PNO) broke out after the PNO coerced villages for speedboats and militia recruits.[^cite_note-216]&#x20;



View of the Kalay-Falam Road

In late 2022, [Chin State](493366.md) resistance forces used drones in a week-long siege of an outpost in [Falam Township](15141612.md), killing 74% of the junta forces stationed, but failing to take the outpost against aerial bombardments.[^cite_note-217] In February 2023, [CNA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_National_Army) captured [Thantlang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thantlang) police station and took control of the town.[^cite_note-218] In [Kachin State](493369.md), the [Shanni Nationalities Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanni_Nationalities_Army) (SNA) became more actively allied with the junta as conflict between SNA and the KIA grew. In August, the SNA and the [Myanmar Army](9503548.md) set fire to hundreds of homes in Kachin state forcing KIA to withdraw from the area.[^cite_note-219]&#x20;

Chin forces also targeted convoys on roads within the state. In March 2023, combined Chin resistance consisting of [CNA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_National_Army), [CNDF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_National_Defence_Force), and [CDFs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinland_Defense_Force) conducted multiple ambushes on a regime convoy between [Kalay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalay), [Falam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falam) and [Hakha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakha) capturing and destroyed multiple armoured vehicles. The NUG awarded the combined Chin forces 400 million [kyat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burmese_kyat) for seizing two armoured vehicles.[^cite_note-awardforconvoy-220] The following day, the groups attacked another junta convoy carrying 80 troops on the road between [Matupi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matupi,_Myanmar) and [Paletwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paletwa), killing over 30 junta soldiers.[^cite_note-221] In April, CNDF attacked a junta base on the Kalay-Falam road near Varr, [Falam Township](15141612.md), killing eleven regime soldiers and capturing fourteen.[^cite_note-222]&#x20;

##### *Lowland attacks*

In November 2022, resistance in [Bago Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bago_Region) increased. In [Monyo Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monyo_Township), western Bago Region, the PDF attacked a police building using [cluster bombs](79745.md).[^cite_note-223] In eastern Bago, 15 junta soldiers were killed in a Bago PDF raid on a police station in [Yedashe Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yedashe_Township).[^cite_note-224] Thousands of civilians also fled [Shwegyin Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shwegyin_Township) as joint KNLA and NUG-led resistance forces seized three military outposts.[^cite_note-225]&#x20;



A rural area near [Mawlaik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mawlaik), Sagaing Region

In early December, a video of [PDF](67819275.md) forces beating and shooting a woman dead emerged on social media. The NUG Ministry of Defence said that the incident happened in June in [Tamu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamu,_Myanmar), Sagaing and that they were investigating the incident after detaining the perpetrators involved.[^cite_note-226]&#x20;

In early January 2023, PDF groups in [Kani Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kani_Township), Sagaing Region attacked junta supply ships, killing at least 25 soldiers. The junta increasingly used waterways for supplies, avoiding roadways in resistance-held areas.[^cite_note-227] In April 2023, a combined PDF force from nearby townships seized the Tower Taing hill base Kani Township, killing 30 junta soldiers and seizing weapons.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229]&#x20;

In early 2023, the Mandalay PDF announced their intentions to ramp up military operations.[^cite_note-230] Alongside the TNLA, they engaged in a series of intense clashes with the junta forces in [Nawnghkio Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nawnghkio_Township) near the [Shan](493376.md)-[Mandalay](2357817.md) border, killing at least 75 junta soldiers and wounding 60 others.[^cite_note-231] A combined force of at least 900 junta and pro-junta militia troops attacked resistance positions with the help of artillery attacks and airstrikes during the clashes but were forced to retreat.[^cite_note-232]&#x20;

##### *Urban attacks*

In 2023, attacks in urban areas increased. In March 2023, the [urban guerilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_guerrilla_warfare) group Urban Owls assassinated Minn Tayzar Nyunt Tin, a legal and money-laundering aide to the junta with links to former Air Force commander General [Myat Hein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myat_Hein), in [Thanlyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanlyin), Yangon. Minn Tayzar Nyunt Tin helped draft the repressive Cyber Security Law, which was seen as violating digital rights, privacy, and freedom of expression.[^cite_note-UrbanOwls1-233]&#x20;

#### Junta retaliation and atrocities

See also: [Killing of Saw Tun Moe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Saw_Tun_Moe) and [Hpakant massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hpakant_massacre)

In October 2022, battles and skirmishes increased as the junta committed several civilian atrocities. On 21 October, junta forces [decapitated Saw Tun Moe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Saw_Tun_Moe), a high school teacher from [Thit Nyi Naung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thit_Nyi_Naung), and impaled his head on a [NUG](67413816.md)-administered school's spiked gate after burning and looting [Taung Myint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taung_Myint) village in [Magway Region](493360.md).[^cite_note-234]&#x20;



[Mogaung Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mogaung_Township), east of [Hpakant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hpakant)

On 23 October, over 80 people were killed by [an airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hpakant_massacre) in [Hpakant Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hpakant_Township), Northern Myanmar, during an anniversary celebration for the [Kachin Independence Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kachin_Independence_Organisation) (KIO). At least 80 civilians were killed, making it the deadliest attack on civilians since the start of the renewed civil war.[^cite_note-cbsnews-235] The junta denied civilian casualties while the United Nations condemned the attack.[^cite_note-236]&#x20;

In November 2022, the junta continued burning villages in [Sagaing Region](493361.md), including the home village of [Cardinal](6221.md) [Charles Maung Bo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Maung_Bo), the head of the [Catholic Church in Myanmar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Myanmar).[^cite_note-237] Junta soldiers also hid in civilian trucks impersonating workers to ambush local defence forces in [Shwebo Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shwebo_Township).[^cite_note-238]&#x20;

On 2 February 2023, [Min Aung Hlaing](38278640.md) imposed martial law in 37 townships with resistance activity, affecting millions of residents.[^cite_note-239]&#x20;

##### *Scorched earth tactics*

Further information: [Tar Taing massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tar_Taing_massacre), [Pinlaung massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinlaung_massacre), and [Pazigyi massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pazigyi_massacre)

In November 2022, the [dry season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Myanmar) allowed the greater use of the [Myanmar Air Force](9484818.md) to weaken the resistance forces' ability to maintain strategic positions and outposts. Aerial bombardment, helicopter raids and artillery strikes typically followed skirmishes once junta ground forces sustained substantial losses and retreated. Once the entrapped forces were relieved by aerial support, they would engage in [scorched earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scorched_earth) tactics. World War Two veterans described the destruction as worse than that of the [Burma campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burma_campaign) of [World War II](32927.md) due to the deliberate targeting of civilian villages.[^cite_note-240] The heavy use of air forces came alongside a decrease in the junta's ability to fight on the ground. During the week of 21 November, repeated junta air attacks along the [Sagaing](493361.md)-[Kachin](493369.md) border killed 80 and disrupted supply chains between the two resistance regions.[^cite_note-241] The junta's [scorched earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scorched_earth) campaign stretched across northern Myanmar, burning bases and villages they could no longer defend.[^cite_note-242] Thousands of residents fled during the campaign as hundreds of homes were destroyed.[^cite_note-243] In early 2023, one scorched earth push by the junta aimed to resecure the [Letpadaung Copper Mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Letpadaung_Copper_Mine) in [Salingyi Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salingyi_Township) for Chinese foreign workers planning to leave for their holidays.[^cite_note-244]&#x20;

On 23 February 2023, army troops launched a new military offensive in Sagaing, raiding and pillaging villages at the confluence of the [Irrawaddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irrawaddy_River) and [Mu Rivers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mu_River). During the offensive, troops from the 99th Light Infantry Division executed at least 17 villagers during the [Tar Taing massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tar_Taing_massacre).[^cite_note-\:1-245]&#x20;

### Temporary stalemate (November 2022 – September 2023)

#### November 2022 Arakan ceasefire



Fields in [Maungdaw Township](15160625.md), northern Rakhine State

On 26 November 2022, the [Arakan Army](38106209.md) and the junta agreed to a temporary ceasefire starting on 27 November. The ceasefire was brokered by [Yōhei Sasakawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y%C5%8Dhei_Sasakawa) of the [Nippon Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nippon_Foundation). Arakan Army spokespeople maintained that they agreed to the ceasefire for humanitarian reasons, as opposed to international pressure. The Arakan Army did not withdraw from fortifications held at the time of the ceasefire.[^cite_note-246] Junta spokespeople said that this was the first step towards a permanent ceasefire with the Arakan Army.[^cite_note-247] As of mid-December, tensions remained high with forces from both sides remaining in deployment within northern [Rakhine State](493373.md).[^cite_note-248]&#x20;

#### Subsequent new fronts

On 30 November, the military launched a major assault on the [Kokang](1635498.md) [Myanmar National Democratic Alliance Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myanmar_National_Democratic_Alliance_Army) using heavy weapons on a base near [Chinshwehaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinshwehaw) by the [Chinese border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Myanmar_border). This assault continued into 2 December, reportedly sending 500 junta soldiers.[^cite_note-249]&#x20;

The military continued its campaign in northern Shan State against the [Ta'ang National Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ta%27ang_National_Liberation_Army) (TNLA). On 7 December 2022, the junta launched a ground offensive on the TNLA in the Battle of [Namhsan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namhsan_Township) using aerial bombs.[^cite_note-250] After six days of fighting, the TNLA captured four villages from junta's control, killing 70 soldiers and capturing 28. On 17 December, the junta retreated, claiming that they had reached an agreement with TNLA, and that they intended to target the PDF forces and attacked the TNLA in mistake. The TNLA rejected the statement.[^cite_note-251] Continued clashes in late December forced over a thousand civilians to flee to [Mogok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mogok).[^cite_note-252]&#x20;

#### 2023 guerrilla attacks



Burning Car in [Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashio), April 2023

According to analysts in early 2023, the civil war was in a stalemate. Despite several successful engagements, there was still a significant disparity in power between the joint resistance forces and the junta. The PDF and EAOs faced resource constraints as they primarily relied on donations for funding and underground channels to acquire arms.[^cite_note-noendinsight-70] The resistance also increasingly used coordinated drone attacks, such as on 27 August 2023, when 11 resistance groups jointly conducted drone strikes in [Sagaing Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sagaing_Township), killing 17 soldiers.[^cite_note-253]&#x20;

In early April, the [Kawthoolei Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawthoolei_Army) (KTLA) launched an offensive on the Karen State [Border Guard Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Border_Guard_Force) (BGF)-held [Shwe Kokko](59825711.md). After the reported capture of 5 BGF bases,[^cite_note-254] by 8 April, the offensive began to stall. After junta/BGF counterattacks, the KTLA was forced to retreat, receiving heavy losses. The KNU stated that they did not approve of these attacks, nor that they would accept the KTLA in their territory.[^cite_note-255]&#x20;

On 19 June 2023, the Urban Owls guerilla group assassinated former air force major Ye Khaing, the operations director of [Yangon International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangon_International_Airport) in [Mingaladon Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mingaladon_Township), Yangon. Ye Khaing was allegedly detaining anti-junta activists at the airport.[^cite_note-256] Urban Owls also claimed that Ye Khaing was a confidante of [Steven Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Law_\(businessman\)).[^cite_note-257]&#x20;

In late June 2023, a combined resistance force of PDF and KNLA took control of the [National Highway 8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_8_\(Myanmar\)) in [Mon State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mon_State), installing checkpoints and arresting junta personnel.[^cite_note-258] The [Ye Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ye_Township)-based resistance group Ye Belu launched successive guerrilla attacks, including an attack in [Lamaing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamaing,_Ye),[^cite_note-259] assassinating a [Pyusawhti militia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyusawhti_militias) leader in [Duya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duya,_Myanmar),[^cite_note-260] and ambushing junta army convoya.[^cite_note-261] The attacks caused a breakdown in junta administration throughout Ye Township.[^cite_note-262]&#x20;

On 10 August 2023, junta forces clashed with a coalition of several rebel groups at [Thandaung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thandaung), near [Nay Pyi Taw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nay_Pyi_Taw).[^cite_note-263] On 15 September, they carried out the first documented resistance drone attack, targeting the Aye Lar military base near [Nay Pyi Taw International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nay_Pyi_Taw_International_Airport) with 2 makeshift bombs.[^cite_note-264]&#x20;

On August and September 2023, the [Zomi Revolutionary Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zomi_Revolutionary_Army) (ZRA) raided 2 [Chinland Defense Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinland_Defense_Force) (CDF) outposts in [Tonzang Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonzang_Township), killing 2 CDF soldiers, continuing clashes between the groups which have been occurring since 2021 despite the ZRA issuing a public statement of support for anti-junta resistance in 2021.[^cite_note-zomi-15]&#x20;

#### 2023 monsoon offensives

Further information: [Operation Kanaung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Kanaung)

In August 2023, the NUG claimed that 3,012 junta troops were killed between January and July 2023.[^cite_note-first2023-265] In Kayah state alone, 667 military junta troops and 99 resistance members were killed.[^cite_note-first2023-265] In a September interview, [Duwa Lashi La](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duwa_Lashi_La) claimed that resistance forces had taken effective control of about 60% of Myanmar's territory.[^cite_note-266]&#x20;

In early June 2023, a coalition force led by [KNLA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_National_Liberation_Army) ambushed junta forces at Don Tha Mi bridge checkpoints on the border of [Karen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayin_State) and [Mon States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mon_State), inflicting heavy casualties.[^cite_note-KNLAJune-267] They also raided the police station and junta offices in [Kyain Seikgyi Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyain_Seikgyi_Township).[^cite_note-KNLAJune-267]&#x20;

In [Kayah State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayah_State) on 13 June 2023, the [Karenni National People's Liberation Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karenni_National_People%27s_Liberation_Front) (KNPLF), broke the ceasefire they had since 2009 and openly defected to anti-junta forces. The KNPLF began attacking Burmese military positions, joining forces with [KA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karenni_Army), [KNDF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karenni_Nationalities_Defence_Force), KNLA, and PDF,[^cite_note-268] and seizing junta outposts in the [Battle of Mese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Mese). The combined forces took over [Mese Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mese_Township) in Eastern [Kayah State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayah_State).[^cite_note-269] 430 soldiers of the Light Infantry Battalion, including their commander, surrendered to the resistance.[^cite_note-270] Later in July, KNLA forces and allies captured the Lat Khat Taung hill junta base, which the junta failed to recapture.[^cite_note-271]&#x20;

From July to September 2023, the [Ta'ang National Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ta%27ang_National_Liberation_Army) and the [Mandalay People's Defence Force](67819275.md) jointly conducted [Operation Kanaung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Kanaung) against junta forces in the [Mandalay Region](2357817.md). Over that period, 76 junta soldiers were killed, 19 were wounded, and a large amount of weapons and ammunition were seized.[^cite_note-272][^cite_note-273]&#x20;

### Operation 1027 and concurrent offensives (October 2023 – January 2024)

#### Operation 1027 (Phase 1)

Main article: [Operation 1027](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1027)



Captured [Tatmadaw](20394.md) equipment in [Kawlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawlin), November 2023



Map of anti-junta gains from Phase 1 of Operation 1027 as of 19 March 2024

On 27 October 2023, the [Three Brotherhood Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Brotherhood_Alliance) initiated [Operation 1027](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1027), targeting the junta's checkpoints and bases near [Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashio) and trade posts near [Chinshwehaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinshwehaw).[^cite_note-274] Chinshwehaw fell into ethnic armies' hands.[^cite_note-275] Within days, the coalition forces captured 57 bases, and the junta responded with aerial bombardments.[^cite_note-276]&#x20;

[Nawnghkio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nawnghkio) fell under TNLA and Mandalay [PDF](67819275.md) control[^cite_note-277] Combined AA and KIA forces captured Gangdau Yang base on the [Myitkyina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myitkyina)-[Bhamo](2150645.md) road.[^cite_note-278] The junta acknowledged having lost control of three towns in Northern [Shan State](493376.md), including [Pang Hseng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pang_Hseng).[^cite_note-279] TNLA, MNDAA, and AA declared control over four towns, including [Hsenwi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hsenwi).[^cite_note-280]&#x20;



[TNLA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ta%27ang_National_Liberation_Army) captured the Shan State towns of [Namhkam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namhkam,_Shan_State)

In early November, TNLA forces seized [Namhkam, Shan State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namhkam,_Shan_State) and nearby bridges and road gates.[^cite_note-281] [Kawlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawlin) also fell to the coalition, marking the first [district-level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Myanmar) capital to fall to the resistance.[^cite_note-kawlin-282] The coalition took [Khampat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khampat), [Kunlong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunlong) and [Monekoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monekoe) across northern Myanmar, re-establishing local government functions after securing towns.[^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285] They also took the strategic Goktwin bridge near the [Goteik viaduct](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goteik_viaduct) on the main [Mandalay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandalay)-China highway.[^cite_note-286]&#x20;

Through December, the TNLA seized [Namhsan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namhsan) and [Mantong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mantong,_Myanmar) taking over the entire [Pa Laung Self-Administered Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pa_Laung_Self-Administered_Zone).[^cite_note-namhsan-287][^cite_note-288]&#x20;

The [Shan State Progress Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shan_State_Progress_Party) (SSPP) and the [Restoration Council of Shan State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Restoration_Council_of_Shan_State) (RCSS) declared a truce between their respective armies in response to the Operation.[^cite_note-289]&#x20;

In early December, the Tatmadaw allegedly asked China to pressure the [Three Brotherhood Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Brotherhood_Alliance) to stop Operation 1027.[^cite_note-290] On 11 December, China helped hold peace talks between the Tatmadaw and various rebel groups.[^cite_note-291] The Brotherhood Alliance announced on 13 December that these talks "lasted only 10 minutes" and vowed to continue fighting.[^cite_note-292]&#x20;

In late January 2024, *[BBC News](1139893.md)* said that the "bloody two-year stalemate" of the civil war appeared to "have been broken" with the success of the offense of the three ethnic armies in Operation 1027.[^cite_note-BBC-losing-24-1-2024-76]&#x20;

##### *Arakan Army offensive*

Further information: [Rakhine offensive (2023–present)](76441089.md)



[Paletwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paletwa) seen from the [Kaladan River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaladan_River), 2015

On the morning of 13 November 2023, as part of [Operation 1027](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1027), the [Arakan Army](38106209.md) (AA) attacked two [Border Guard Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Border_Guard_Police) stations in [Rathedaung Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rathedaung_Township), breaking the Rakhine State Ceasefire Agreement between the junta and the Arakan Army. Dong Paik camp was captured by 6\:30 am.[^cite_note-rakceasefire2-293] On 14 November, the junta had already abandoned around 40 outposts in [Rakhine state](493373.md) after attacks by the Arakan Army, but few came under their immediate control.[^cite_note-294] Dozens of Myanmar security officers surrendered to the Arakan Army the following day.[^cite_note-295]&#x20;

On 14 November, the Arakan Army launched an offensive in [Paletwa Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paletwa_Township) in neighbouring [Chin State](493366.md). The Arakan Army accused the Tatmadaw of using chemical weapons during the ensuing battles.[^cite_note-296]&#x20;

The following night, the Arakan Army launched an attack on [Pauktaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pauktaw), seizing the [township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pauktaw_Township) police station. By the next morning, the Arakan Army had taken control of the town. The junta sent two [helicopter gunships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helicopter_gunships) alongside naval support to fire back, including at civilian housing, with heavy machine gun fire. Pauktaw's proximity to the Rakhine state capital, [Sittwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sittwe), posed a threat to the junta.[^cite_note-297] Junta forces detained about 100 residents who were unable to flee, and positioned themselves to surround the town, using two navy ships to blockade the harbour.[^cite_note-298]&#x20;

On 6 December, the Arakan Army would capture a major military base in the township.[^cite_note-299]&#x20;

##### *Battle of Laukkai*

Main article: [Battle of Laukkai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Laukkai)



[China–Myanmar border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Myanmar_border) gate near [Laukkai](24155033.md)

In late November and December 2023, the Myanmar National Democratic Alliance Army (MNDAA) closed in on [Laukkai](24155033.md), the capital of the [Kokang Self-Administered Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kokang_Self-Administered_Zone). They seized several strategic positions from the junta forces during the ensuing [Battle of Laukkai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Laukkai).[^cite_note-300] MNDAA forces attacked junta bases around the city in early December.[^cite_note-301] On 26 December, ninety junta soldiers surrendered to the MNDAA.[^cite_note-302] and, two days later, the artillery shelling of Laukkai would stop as the city mostly fell under MNDAA control.[^cite_note-303] On 5 January 2024, the MNDAA seized control of the junta battalion headquarters in Laukkai and gained full control of the city.[^cite_note-laukkai-73]&#x20;

A few days later, the Three Brotherhood Alliance claimed it had captured the towns of [Kutkai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kutkai) and [Theinni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theinni) on 8 January after seizing junta military posts in the towns, including the headquarters of the [16th Military Operations Command](9503548.md#Military_Operations_Commands_\(MOC\)) in Theinni.[^cite_note-304] On 23 January, the Tatmadaw sentenced three of the brigadier generals who surrendered at Laukkai to death and the other three to [life imprisonment](148681.md), under [military law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_justice).[^cite_note-305]&#x20;

In the wake of these gains and the fall of Laukkai, on 12 January, China announced that it had negotiated another [ceasefire](592591.md) between the junta and the 3BA, known as the "Haigeng Agreement".[^cite_note-306] The two sides agreed to disengage personnel and to not compromise the safety of Chinese border residents.[^cite_note-2024cease-307] However, the following day, the TNLA reported that the junta had broken their agreement with airstrikes in [Lashio Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashio_Township) and [Kyaukme Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukme_Township).[^cite_note-308]&#x20;

##### *Tatmadaw defensive response*



Two ATR-42 of the Myanmar Air Force at [Mawlamyine Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mawlamyine_Airport)

*[The Irrawaddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Irrawaddy)* reported on 22 November 2023 that the Tatmadaw was preparing 14,000 soldiers for the defence of [Naypyitaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naypyitaw), including by moving troops from other regions to the capital and mobilizing civil servants into the military. These preparations started soon after Operation 1027 was launched against the Tatmadaw. In addition, the Tatmadaw was preparing 10,000 troops for the defence of [Mandalay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandalay), [Bago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bago,_Myanmar) and [Yangon](57404.md). There were also fortification works beginning, with Naypyitaw police stations "also preparing concrete blocks, sandbags and other materials needed to transform into defensive bases in just a few days".[^cite_note-309]&#x20;

[Ronan Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronan_Lee), a professor at [Loughborough University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loughborough_University), stated that the recent strategic reversals, nationwide territorial losses and economic decline meant momentum had strongly shifted away from Myanmar's junta, and the junta "may now be in a death spiral".[^cite_note-310]&#x20;

#### Concurrent operations

Main articles: [Operation 1107](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1107), [Operation 1111](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1111), and [Operation Taungthaman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Taungthaman)

[Tatmadaw](20394.md) convoy near [Pyin Oo Lwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyin_Oo_Lwin), October 2023

Operation 1027 was supported by several concurrent operations by other anti-junta groups elsewhere in the country, including in the eastern regions [Shan State](493376.md) and [Kayah State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayah_State). In northern Shan State, the KNLA and [PDF](67819275.md) clashed with the Tatmadaw around the town of [Kawkareik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawkareik) in late October 2023.[^cite_note-311] In [Mese Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mese_Township) (part of Kayah State), the KNPLF, KA and KNDF launched a joint military operation called [Operation 1107](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1107) that captured several border posts starting on 7 November.[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313] Four days later, they launched the major [Operation 1111](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1111) against [Loikaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loikaw), the capital of Kayah State.[^cite_note-314] The military operations in Kayah displaced tens of thousands of civilians, especially from Loikaw.[^cite_note-200d-315] After over a month of heavy fighting, rebel forces had won control of 85% of the capital.[^cite_note-316] Nonetheless, fighting continued into January.[^cite_note-karepekon-317] By late January, however, the offensive on Loikaw had mostly stalled.[^cite_note-318]&#x20;

Other anti-junta forces launched [Operation Taungthaman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Taungthaman) in [Madaya Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madaya_Township), Mandalay Region. On 13 November, fighting erupted in Kinn Village, eastern Madaya Township between the TNLA and the junta, who responded with air and artillery strikes and later burned the village down.[^cite_note-319] By 28 November, PDF and TNLA forces captured a junta base in the township.[^cite_note-320] The TNLA additionally supported the operations with attacks in [Nawnghkio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nawnghkio_Township) and [Kyaukme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukme_Township) Townships in southern [Shan State](493376.md) to cut off junta reinforcements.[^cite_note-321]&#x20;

#### Chin offensive

Main article: [Chin theater § 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_theater#2023)



[Kennedy Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kennedy_Peak_\(Myanmar\)), Chin State

On the morning of 13 November 2023, after two days of fighting, the [Chin National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_National_Army) (CNA) and local [Chinland Defense Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinland_Defense_Force) (CDF) units, captured the town of [Rikhawdar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rikhawdar) on the [India–Myanmar border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India%E2%80%93Myanmar_border).[^cite_note-322] This marked the first town captured by resistance forces in Chin State since the coup. At least 40 junta soldiers and police officers fled to the Indian state of [Mizoram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mizoram), where they surrendered and were eventually repatriated.[^cite_note-323] The [Chin National Defence Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_National_Defence_Force) (CNDF), took a junta camp on the Mizoram border two days later.[^cite_note-324] The following week on 21 November, local Zoland PDF units seized a military base on [Kennedy Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kennedy_Peak_\(Myanmar\)), the second highest mountain in Chin State.[^cite_note-325] Over the next week, CNA and its allies captured Lailenpi[^cite_note-326] and [Rezua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rezua) in [Matupi Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matupi_Township).[^cite_note-327]&#x20;

On 6 December 2023, the [Chin National Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_National_Front) ratified the Chinland Constitution, proclaiming the state of [Chinland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinland).[^cite_note-328]&#x20;

On 17 January 2024, the Taingen camp on the [Falam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falam) road to the Indian border was captured, with Chin resistance forces seizing arms and ammunition.[^cite_note-329] On 20 January 2024, after more than 600 junta soldiers and refugees crossed the [India–Myanmar border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India%E2%80%93Myanmar_border), the [Government of India](553883.md) announced a plan to fence the entire border.[^cite_note-330]&#x20;

### New conflict landscape (January 2024–May 2024)

Following the fall of [Laukkai](24155033.md) and the junta facing serious threats by the [Three Brotherhood Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Brotherhood_Alliance), the war has turned into a more multipolar landscape with borderlands being seized by powerful anti-junta groups with significant implications for the [foreign relations of Myanmar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Myanmar). Groups not a part of the alliance took advantage of the situation, but remained constrained by both tense relations and limited coordination.[^cite_note-iissmar24-331]&#x20;

#### Rakhine offensive intensifies

Main article: [Rakhine offensive (2023–present)](76441089.md)



Map of gains made by the Arakan Army since 2023

On 8 January 2024, the [Arakan Army](38106209.md) continued their offensive and captured the Taung Shey Taung base and its 200 junta soldiers in [Kyauktaw Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyauktaw_Township), Rakhine State. On 15 January, the Arakan Army seized [Paletwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paletwa), a strategic town for Indo-Myanmar infrastructure projects, and the entire township in Chin State.[^cite_note-aakt-332][^cite_note-333] A week later, the Arakan Army captured the town of [Pauktaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pauktaw) in Rakhine State concluding a three-month battle.[^cite_note-334]&#x20;

On 3 February 2024, as the clashes between Arakan Army and Tatmadaw increased in Rakhine, mortar shells and several bullets reportedly landed in Bangladesh's territory, which injured some local residents near [Ukhia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukhia_Upazila), Cox's Bazar.[^cite_note-DS-335] At least 229 Myanmar [Border Guard Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Border_Guard_Police) (BGP) personnel entered Bangladesh seeking refuge from AA, where the [Border Guard Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Border_Guard_Bangladesh) (BGB) disarmed them.[^cite_note-336]&#x20;

Between 4 and 6 February, the Arakan Army launched attacks on Rakhine BGP outposts in Maungdaw Township, later alleging without providing evidence that the [Arakan Rohingya Salvation Army](52247136.md) and [Rohingya Solidarity Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rohingya_Solidarity_Organisation) (RSO) fought alongside the Rakhine BGP.[^cite_note-RSO_Junta-337] The RSO denounced AA's accusations and the AA labeling them as "[Bengalis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengalis)" among other issues.[^cite_note-RSO_Junta-337] Later in February, when the junta began conscripting largely non-citizen [displaced](427408.md) [Rohingyas](1918632.md) living in [Kyaukphyu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukphyu), the AA urged Rohingya people to flee into AA-controlled areas.[^cite_note-338]&#x20;

##### *Fall of Mrauk U*

Main article: [Rakhine offensive (2023–present) § #Fall of Mrauk U](76441089.md##Fall_of_Mrauk_U)



Mrauk U ancient temples in 2017

The Arakan Army captured most remaining Tatmadaw bases in [Minbya Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minbya_Township) by 6 February 2024.[^cite_note-339] It captured [Kyauktaw](15160309.md) the next day and continued fighting in [Mrauk U](2636746.md) and [Ramree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramree).[^cite_note-340] The Tatmadaw abandoned [Myebon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myebon) to reinforce [Kyaukphyu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukphyu) on 9 February, leaving ammunition behind in their rush and abandoning the southern township of [Mrauk-U District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mrauk-U_District).[^cite_note-341]&#x20;

On 10 February 2024, the Arakan Army took the district capital town of Mrauk U, completing their control over the township. During the battle, three [Myanmar Navy](9502915.md) landing craft were reportedly sunk.[^cite_note-mrauku-75] Over the following week, the Arakan Army consolidated control over the district, capturing [Myebon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myebon) on 15 February[^cite_note-342] and capturing the remainder of [Minbya Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minbya_Township) on 28 February.[^cite_note-343]&#x20;

#### New Shan State combatants



Southern Shan state mountains near [Hopong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hopong)

The southern [Shan state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shan_state) theatre also re-emerged when the Tatamadaw and the [Pa-O National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pa-O_National_Army) (PNA) attempted to confiscate the [Pa-O National Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pa-O_National_Liberation_Army) (PNLA)'s weapons on 20 January.[^cite_note-PNWei-344] The PNLA, supported by KNDF and PDF forces, attacked [Hsi Hseng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hsi_Hseng) and captured it on 26 January.[^cite_note-345]&#x20;

Local activists reported that junta shelling killed 40 civilians in the [Pa-O Self-Administered Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pa-O_Self-Administered_Zone).[^cite_note-346] Towards the end of February, clashes between junta/PNA forces and PNLA forces broke out east of [Hopong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hopong)[^cite_note-347] and along the [Taunggyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taunggyi)-[Loilem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loilem) road.[^cite_note-348] The junta regained control of Hsi Hseng by late March,[^cite_note-Intersection-349] amid accusations of using [chemical bombs](27179600.md).[^cite_note-350]&#x20;
