Between 26 and 27 March the MNDAA and the [Shan State Army (SSPP)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shan_State_Army_\(SSPP\)) clashed in [Hseni Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hseni_Township) after the alleged MNDAA bombing of SSPP camps.[^cite_note-351] On 27 May, after a month of warnings, the TNLA began blockading several villages in [Namhkam Township](45225213.md) and detaining SSPP soldiers stationed in those villages. In response, the SSPP withdrew from camps in [Hsenwi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hsenwi), [Kutkai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kutkai), and [Pang Hseng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pang_Hseng).[^cite_note-352]&#x20;

The [United Nations](31769.md) voiced deep concern over renewed fighting in eastern Myanmar and allegations that the military employed heavy weapons against civilians and abducted children to join its ranks. Its special advisers highlighted the responsibility of the military to protect all people.[^cite_note-353]&#x20;

#### Junta counteroffensive in Anyar

Main article: [Dry Zone theater § 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dry_Zone_theater#2024)



Local People's Defense Forces guard an anti-SAC junta protest in [Kani Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kani_Township)

Tatmadaw forces recaptured the district capital of [Kawlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawlin) on 10 February after almost 10 days of fighting.[^cite_note-354] Junta forces [razed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scorched_earth) the town, destroying the majority of homes in Kawlin and surrounding villages.[^cite_note-355] On 22 February, junta forces attempted to recapture the town of [Maw Luu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maw_Luu) from the [KIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kachin_Independence_Army) and [ABSDF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_Burma_Students%27_Democratic_Front).[^cite_note-356] On 14 March, junta forces took the village of Kampani, [Kalay Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalay_Township) to weaken anti-junta attacks on [Kalay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalay).[^cite_note-357] Despite this campaign, on 16 March resistance forces captured the [Pyusawhti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyusawhti_militias)-controlled village of Kyaung Taik north of Kalay.[^cite_note-358]&#x20;

While the junta launched its counteroffensives, the allied resistance launched an offensive to capture [Kani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kani,_Myanmar), capturing around 80% of the town by 7 March.[^cite_note-359] After almost 10 days of fighting, by 15 March, rebels were forced to retreat after overwhelming junta resistance.[^cite_note-360]&#x20;

On 4 April 2024, the PDF launched an unprecedented drone attack against Aye Lar Air Base, the main Tatmadaw headquarters, and Min Aung Hlaing's residence in the capital, [Naypyidaw](2270051.md). Almost 30 drones were deployed; junta forces claimed 7 were shot down[^cite_note-361] while [Myawaddy TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myawaddy_TV) said 13 fixed-wing drones were shot down and there were no casualties or damage to property. NUG claimed the attack was "a success".[^cite_note-362] On 12 April, the local People's Defense Forces claimed that they killed over a dozen junta soldiers in another attack on Aye Lar Air Base.[^cite_note-363]&#x20;

On 19 April junta forces launched a counteroffensive to retake Shwe Pyi Aye, [Homalin Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homalin_Township), after it was captured in November 2023.[^cite_note-364]&#x20;

#### Junta control of Karen weakens



Journalist in front of a destroyed building, [Karen State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_State)

After Operation 1027 and the [Battle of Kawkareik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Kawkareik) in October 2023, the KNLA continued to make gains throughout [Karen State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_State), [Mon State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mon_State), [Bago Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bago_Region), and [Tanintharyi Region](15162240.md).[^cite_note-365] On 29 January 2024, KNLA and PDF forces shot down a Tatmadaw [Eurocopter AS365](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurocopter_AS365), killing Brigadier General Aye Min Naung of the 44th Light Infantry Division.[^cite_note-366] In late February, PDF and KNLA forces took most of [Myitta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myitta), Tanintharyi Region.[^cite_note-367] By 29 February, KNLA and aligned forces captured half of [Kawkareik township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawkareik_township), gaining fire control over the [Myawaddy-Kawkareik highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AH1).[^cite_note-368] The KNLA continued gaining pushing east on the highway,[^cite_note-369] capturing the Hpu Lu Gyi camp, south of Myawaddy, after a "five minute fight". This camp held both strategic and moral significance as the historic staging point for attacks on [Manerplaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Manerplaw) and [Kawmoora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Kawmoora) after the junta captured it in 1990.[^cite_note-370] The next day, KNLA forces captured a junta base in [Kyaikdon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaikdon).[^cite_note-371] Fighting also broke out in KNLA 7th Brigade territory near Methawaw, forcing a junta retreat.[^cite_note-372]&#x20;

Tensions also rose between the junta and the Karen State [Border Guard Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Border_Guard_Force) (BGF), who refused orders to engage in battle and withdrew from their bases in [Papun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papun).[^cite_note-373] On 23 January, deputy commander-in-chief [Soe Win](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soe_Win_\(general\)) met with Karen BGF leader Colonel [Saw Chit Thu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saw_Chit_Thu)[^cite_note-374] The Karen BGF announced they would no longer accept salaries from the junta, and would remain "neutral" in the conflict. Later, on 6 March, the Karen BGF announced it would rename itself to the "[Karen National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_National_Army)".[^cite_note-375]&#x20;

#### Kachin conflict escalates

While the [KIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kachin_Independence_Army) is a very close military and political partner of the Three Brotherhood Alliance, being part of the [Northern Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Alliance_\(Myanmar\)), it was not affected by the [Chinese-brokered ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1027#12%E2%80%9313_January). Combined forces of the KIA, ABSDF, and Kachin PDF captured the town of [Mongmit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongmit) on 19 January and neighboring [Mabein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mabein) on the 20th. The next day, they captured the strategically significant Man Wein Gyi base near [Ruili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruili).[^cite_note-kiab-376] The junta recaptured Mongmit on 25 January.[^cite_note-kiac-377]&#x20;

The KIA also intensified attacks in Hpakant Township,[^cite_note-kiac-377] capturing a camp southwest of Hpakant on 20 January and[^cite_note-378] threatening the Myitkyina-Hpakant road with the capture of Namtein outpost on 2 February.[^cite_note-379] There were also attacks in [Mansi Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mansi_Township), including the capture of the Si Kham Gyi base, which had been held by the junta for 30 years.[^cite_note-kiab-376]&#x20;

##### *"Operation 0307"*

Main article: [2024 Kachin offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Kachin_offensive)



[China–Myanmar border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Myanmar_border) gate in [Laiza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laiza), 2015

On 7 March the KIA simultaneously launched attacks on over ten junta outposts in eastern Kachin. Fighting primarily took place along the highway between [Bhamo](2150645.md) and the Kachin State capital, Myitkyina, as well as around [Laiza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laiza). The attacks were the beginning of a wider offensive in Kachin State- colloquially termed [Operation 0307](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_0307).[^cite_note-380] Over 8 March, the KIA seized three major junta bases and several outposts, including Hpyun Pyen Bum, the junta's closest forward base to KIA headquarters in Laiza.[^cite_note-381] The KIA and AA defending Laiza alleged that junta airstrikes had landed on the [Chinese side of the border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nabang,_Yingjiang_County), east of Laiza.[^cite_note-382] During the fighting, the leader of the junta-aligned [Lisu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lisu_people) ["Wuyang People's Militia"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lisu_National_Development_Party), U Shwe Min, was killed.[^cite_note-hum-383] By 22 March, the KIA claimed to have captured over 50 military outposts and 13 strategically significant junta bases around the Myitkyina-[Bhamo](2150645.md) Road.[^cite_note-384]&#x20;

In April, the KIA had captured the key Chinese border trade hub of [Lweje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lweje),[^cite_note-1_April-385] cut off a major road to [Hpakant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hpakant),[^cite_note-386] and captured Sezin.[^cite_note-387] and [Hsinbo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hsinbo). Within Hsinbo, they cut off the Bhamo-Myitkyina road and encircled [Bhamo](2150645.md).[^cite_note-388] The KIA then launched simultaneous offensives throughout [Waingmaw Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waingmaw_Township) and around [Sumprabum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumprabum), capturing several junta bases, camps and command centers.[^cite_note-389] By 8 May, they had captured 11 battalion headquarters throughout the state[^cite_note-KIA_8May-390] and captured [Sumprabum Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumprabum_Township).[^cite_note-391]&#x20;

Through the rest of May, the KIA captured [Momauk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Momauk) after junta soldiers retreated,[^cite_note-392] attacked a bridge at the entrance of the state capital [Myitkyina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myitkyina).[^cite_note-393] and made gains in [Mansi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mansi,_Myanmar)[^cite_note-394] and [Tanai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanai,_Myanmar)[^cite_note-395] The KIA took an additional dozen junta bases in Waingmaw Township starting in 20 May, securing their positions.[^cite_note-396]&#x20;

#### Continued Rakhine offensive

Main article: [Rakhine offensive (2023-present) § Continued northern offensives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rakhine_offensive_\(2023-present\)#Continued_northern_offensives)



Mosque in [Buthidaung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buthidaung), 2015

At the same time, the Rakhine offensive continued through March. On 5 March, the Arakan Army captured [Ponnagyun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ponnagyun) and threatened the regional capital [Sittwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sittwe), only 33 kilometres away.[^cite_note-397] On 17 March, continued Arakan Army assaults took Rathedaung and [its township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rathedaung_Township), cutting off Sittwe from the rest of Rakhine by land.[^cite_note-398]&#x20;

On 12 March, after an 85-day battle, the Arakan Army captured the town of [Ramree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramree), taking all of Ramree Island except for the [deep sea port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukphyu_Deep-Sea_Port) city of [Kyaukphyu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukphyu) under the Arakan Army's control.[^cite_note-399]&#x20;

On 24 March, the Arakan Army began an offensive in central Rakhine concurrently with their offensive on Sittwe, launching attacks on the junta's regional headquarters in [Ann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ann,_Myanmar). North of Ann, the Arakan Army launched attacks on neighbouring [Ngape Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ngape_Township) in [Magway Region](493360.md). Ann's location is strategically important as the link between Rakhine and Magway via the Minbu-Ann road through the [Arakan Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arakan_Mountains) and as a gateway preventing AA from attacking southern Rakhine State.[^cite_note-400] Through April, the Arakan Army cut off Ann from neighboring Padein by capturing parts of the Ann-[Minbu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minbu) Highway[^cite_note-401] and captured bases in the hilltops of Ann township.[^cite_note-Ngapali-402]&#x20;

Northern Rakhine offensives saw little territory change until 3 May when the Arakan Army captured the headquarters of the [Border Guard Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Border_Guard_Police) in [Maungdaw Township](15160625.md) at Kyee Kan Pyin,[^cite_note-403] forcing at least 128 junta soldiers to cross the border into Bangladesh.[^cite_note-404] [Buthidaung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buthidaung) and its surrounding [township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buthidaung_Township) fell to the Arakha Army on 18 May.[^cite_note-405] After its capture, [Rohingya](1918632.md) activists accused the Arakan Army of [burning and targeting Rohingya homes in the town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burning_of_Buthidaung), a claim which the Arakan Army denied.[^cite_note-406]&#x20;

On 29 May, the junta and allied [Arakan Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arakan_Liberation_Army) soldiers [killed over 70 villagers in Byian Phyu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byian_Phyu_Massacre) near Sittwe due to suspected Arakan Army sympathies.[^cite_note-407]&#x20;

From late May to early June, the Arakan Army launched attacks on the remainder of [Maungdaw Township](15160625.md). On 16 June, the AA urged the residents of Maungdaw to evacuate the town, warning of their imminent attack.[^cite_note-408] In response, the [Bangladesh Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Navy) deployed warships to the disputed [St. Martin's Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Martin%27s_Island), which had [been shot at several times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spillover_of_the_Myanmar_civil_war_\(2021%E2%80%93present\)) by junta forces.[^cite_note-409] On 4 July, the AA entered Maungdaw, attacking the last junta holdout in the town.[^cite_note-410]&#x20;

#### Capture of Papun and Myawaddy

Main article: [Siege of Myawaddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Myawaddy)



Smoke rising from [Myawaddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myawaddy), April 2024



Civilians fleeing to Thailand during the [siege of Myawaddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Myawaddy), 2024



Thai-Myanmar friendship bridge from Mae Sot, Thailand

On 20 March, the [Karen National Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_National_Liberation_Army) and its PDF allies began to besiege the town of [Papun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papun), the capital of [Hpapun District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hpapun_District), Karen State. Eight days later, the town was captured, with fighting moving to the hills outside the town.[^cite_note-hpa-80]&#x20;

After a prolonged [siege](26905.md) and several days of negotiations, on 5 April over 600 junta soldiers and their families in [Myawaddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myawaddy) surrendered to the KNU and withdrew across the border to [Mae Sot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mae_Sot),[^cite_note-Myawaddy-411] leaving only the 275th Light Infantry Battalion (LIB) near the western entrance to defend the town. By 10 April, KNLA and PDF troops captured the LIB base with the 200 LIB soldiers withdrawing to the 2nd Thai-Myanmar Friendship Bridge on the border. This prompted Thailand to deploy the [3rd Army](1230371.md#Structure) along the border.[^cite_note-412] The junta sent reinforcements to retake the town, but they were stalled in [Kyondoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyondoe).[^cite_note-413] On 12 April, Thai officials and the KNU confirmed the capture of Myawaddy.[^cite_note-414] The junta retaliated with airstrikes although locals reported no KNLA presence in the streets.[^cite_note-415]&#x20;

Despite the KNLA's major role in the battle, they ceded control of the town to the [Karen National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_National_Army) (KNA) to ensure security[^cite_note-416] and reportedly due to the KNA's role in negotiations for the initial surrender.[^cite_note-417]&#x20;

On 19 April the KNLA attacked the remaining LIB 275th soldiers who were still [holding out](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Last_stand) under the 2nd Thai-Myanmar Friendship Bridge. The junta responded with airstrikes, killing several civilians and forcing the KNLA to delay further attacks.[^cite_note-418][^cite_note-419]&#x20;

##### Continued battle for Myawaddy

Main article: [Siege of Myawaddy § Operation "Aung Zeya"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Myawaddy#Operation_%22Aung_Zeya%22)

After Myawaddy's capture, the junta launched Operation Aung Zeya, a counteroffensive to retake the town led by Light Infantry Division (LID) 55, numbering around 1,000 and reportedly led by the junta's [second-in-command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second-in-command) [Soe Win](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soe_Win_\(general\)). On 16 April, the LID 55 began attempting to cross the [Dawna Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dawna_Range)[^cite_note-420] but was continually intercepted by the KNLA and allies, being forced to retreat and reportedly experiencing heavy losses.[^cite_note-421]&#x20;

Early in the counteroffensive KNLA forces withdrew from most of [Kawkareik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawkareik).[^cite_note-422] On 21 April, a junta convoy was [routed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rout) in [Kawkareik Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawkareik_Township),[^cite_note-423] but junta forces successfully recaptured Kawkareik the next day despite losing vehicles to the KNLA.[^cite_note-JuntaKarenGains-424]&#x20;

On 23 April, the KNA helped the LIB 275th soldiers sheltering under the 2nd Friendship Bridge to retake their base west of Myawaddy.[^cite_note-425] The following day, KNU spokesman stated that they would "temporarily withdraw" from Myawaddy, but vowed to continue guerrilla attacks along the [AH1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Highway_1).[^cite_note-426]&#x20;

The stalled LID 55 advancement moving through the Dawna Range reached the Taw Naw waterfall by 29 April.[^cite_note-427] However, the counteroffensive stalled again, without any major gains the following month. Residents reported that the KNA had helped "hundreds" of junta soldiers to reach Myawaddy through forested paths.[^cite_note-428]&#x20;

#### Mon and Karenni resistance

See also: [Operation 1111](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1111)



Myanmar Air Force bombs a church in [Kayah State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayah_State), May 2024

Karenni resistance continued with [Operation 1111](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1111) aiming to capture [Kayah State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayah_State) and its capital [Loikaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loikaw). In January, they captured [Pekon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pekon) in neighboring Shan State.[^cite_note-karepekon-317] In February, they captured [Shadaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shadaw) after a month-long battle.[^cite_note-429] By 23 March, the Karenni announced that they had captured 65 junta positions, controlled six out of nine towns in Kayah[^cite_note-430] and were in control of nearly 90% of Kayah State.[^cite_note-demoso-79]&#x20;

On 20 January 2024, local resistance forces in [Ye Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ye_Township), Mon State intensified attacks, announcing their intent to capture [Ye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ye,_Myanmar).[^cite_note-431] On 14 February, the [New Mon State Party (Anti-Military Dictatorship)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Mon_State_Party_\(Anti-Military_Dictatorship\)) (MNLA-AMD) split from the non-combatant [Mon National Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mon_National_Liberation_Army) and effectively declared war on the junta.[^cite_note-432] Around 22 March, Mon State resistance groups began vehicle inspections near [Kaleinaung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaleinaung_Subtownship), prompting the junta close roads.[^cite_note-433] MNLA-AMD and allies, would then capture the [Kawt Bein](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Kawt_Bein\&action=edit\&redlink=1) Police Station in [Kawkareik Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawkareik_Township), Karen State.[^cite_note-434] In response, junta forces shelled Kawt Bein [^cite_note-435] and eventually recaptured the area after a battle on 25 April.[^cite_note-436]&#x20;

On 8 April, Mon PDF forces launched drone attacks on the Southeastern Command headquarters in [Mawlamyine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mawlamyine) while junta deputy commander-in-chief [Soe Win](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soe_Win_\(general\)) was present.[^cite_note-437]&#x20;

On 29 May, junta forces launched an offensive to recapture [Loikaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loikaw) from Karenni resistance.[^cite_note-438] Junta columns reached the Loikaw-[Mobye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobye)-[Hsi Hseng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hsi_Hseng) intersection by 3 June before stalling.[^cite_note-Intersection-349] On 25 June, Karenni resistance captured Maesalawng Hill, near [Bawlakhe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bawlakhe), after a six day offensive.[^cite_note-439]&#x20;

##### *Junta counteroffensives in Southern Myanmar*

From 8 May to 20 May, junta forces also conducted an offensive throughout [Thayetchaung Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thayetchaung_Township), the longest of such offensives in [Tanintharyi Region](15162240.md) since the 2021 coup. The offensive displaced 5,000 civilians from 8 villages.[^cite_note-440]&#x20;

Main article: [National Highway 8 offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_8_offensive)

In late May, junta forces captured Pedak outpost on the road between [Dawei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dawei) and [Myeik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myeik,_Myanmar).[^cite_note-441] In early June, around 600 junta soldiers [were sent to recapture areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_8_offensive) of the [Ye - Dawei highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_8_\(Myanmar\)) from Karen, Mon, and PDF forces who had captured the highway in March, The junta used heavy artillery and the resistance warned civilians to avoid the road, accusing the junta of using [human shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_shield) and reinforcements disguised as civilians.[^cite_note-442] On 1 July, junta forces launched an offensive on the Zardi Village Tract of [Yebyu Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yebyu_Township), attempting to recapture areas surrounding the [Dawei Special Economic Zone](38653231.md).[^cite_note-443]&#x20;

### Second phase of Operation 1027 and renewed rebel advances (June 2024–August 2024)



Map of anti-junta gains made during Operation 1027 as of September 2024

In June 2024, the [Three Brotherhood Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Brotherhood_Alliance)'s Haigeng ceasefire with the junta broke down seeing junta battalions destroyed and multiple towns in Shan state and beyond fall to the resistance, culminating in the capture of the Northern [Shan State](493376.md) capital and headquarters of the Northeast Command, [Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashio), in August 2024.[^cite_note-irrmajorgains-444] Outside Shan State, the Myanmar military faced significant losses in Rakhine State where the Arakan Army gained almost full control by August 2024. In Mandalay, the Brotherhood Alliance and other armed groups escalated attacks, getting close to [Mandalay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandalay). Despite these setbacks, the military continued efforts to reassert control, including forming new militias and intensifying conscription across the country.[^cite_note-445] Although 1027 began in Shan State, widespread escalation dramatically shifted the balance of power, leading to EAOs in other areas making dominant gains and crippling the military junta with personnel attrition and historic levels of defections.[^cite_note-ymh-2025-446]&#x20;

#### Operation 1027 (Phase 2)

Main article: [Operation 1027 § Operation 1027 (Phase 2)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1027#Operation_1027_\(Phase_2\))

While it took until June for the Haigeng ceasefire to fully break down, tensions began as early as March. The ceasefire was formalised on 1 March at a meeting in [Kunming](197181.md), with the junta reportedly agreeing to recognise the MNDAA's authority over [Kokang](1635498.md).[^cite_note-iissmar24-331] But as early as 26 March, junta forces attempted to invade MNDAA territory near [Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashio) but was repelled militarily.[^cite_note-447] In response, the Chinese ambassador to Myanmar met with the junta foreign minister to discuss ending the clashes.[^cite_note-448]&#x20;

Tensions began to grow again in Northern Shan State on 9 June after the junta launched airstrikes on TNLA positions in [Mongmit Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongmit_Township). TNLA would accuse the junta of violating the ceasefire.[^cite_note-449] Through mid-June, junta forces amassed near 3BA-territories and destroyed roads in the area[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451]&#x20;

On 23 June, the junta clashed with [Myanmar National Democratic Alliance Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myanmar_National_Democratic_Alliance_Army) (MNDAA) near Lashio.[^cite_note-452] and with the TNLA around [Kyaukme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukme,_Shan_State), [Nawnghkio Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nawnghkio_Township), and [Mogok Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mogok_Township) on 24 June.[^cite_note-453] On 25 June, the TNLA announced that it had restarted [Operation 1027](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1027) in Northern Shan State,[^cite_note-454] and encircled Kyaukme.[^cite_note-455]&#x20;

On 26 June, most of [Nawnghkio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nawnghkio) was captured by PDF and TNLA forces.[^cite_note-456] By 28 June, the TNLA had captured most of the district capital town Kyaukme and continued attacking the junta bases around the town.[^cite_note-Kyaukme-82]&#x20;

#### Southern Rakhine offensives



[Ngapali Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ngapali_Beach), just outside [Thandwe](2969521.md)

Concurrently in early June, the [Arakan Army](38106209.md) made significant gains in southern [Rakhine state](493373.md). On 2 June, clashes erupted on the Thandwe-Taungup highway and near the resort [beach of Ngapali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ngapali_Beach), [Thandwe Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thandwe_Township),[^cite_note-457] trapping resort goers.[^cite_note-458] Over the next week, the fighting moved closer to [Thandwe](2969521.md), forcing the [Thandwe Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thandwe_Airport) to close.[^cite_note-459]&#x20;

On 15 June, the Arakan Army clashed with junta forces near neighbouring [Taungup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taungup)[^cite_note-460] and on the Taungup-[Pandaung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandaung) road.[^cite_note-461] After months of relative peace in the area, heavy clashes broke out outside of [Kyaukphyu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukphyu) on 17 June as junta forces left Danyawaddy naval base.[^cite_note-462]&#x20;

On 23 June, AA forces captured Thandwe Airport, the first airport to be captured by resistance forces since the 2021 coup.[^cite_note-463] The airport's capture was not confirmed for another week, with AA claiming that 400 junta soldiers had died in the battle.[^cite_note-464] The AA began attacking the beach itself on 26 June.[^cite_note-465] On 17 July, AA forces captured Thandwe itself after three weeks of fighting.[^cite_note-Thandwe-466]&#x20;

#### Continued Chin offensive and infighting

Main article: [Chin theater § 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_theater#2024)

Various Chin actors continued the war against the junta in early 2024, with resistance efforts picking up in May. However, in June, the Chin resistance began to engage in conflict with each other as infighting increased in June. Signs of infighting began as early as January when CDFs and CNA clashed with the Maraland Defence Force (MDF), a CBA member.[^cite_note-467] The infighting in Chin State became broadly split along those aligned with the [Chin Brotherhood Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_Brotherhood_Alliance) (CBA) and the [Chinland Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinland_Council), which is dominated by the [Chin National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_National_Army) (CNA). Local [Chinland Defense Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinland_Defense_Force) (CDFs) are generally aligned with the CBA, though some like the CDF-Mara are aligned with the CNA.[^cite_note-468]&#x20;

On 2 May 2024, CBA aligned groups captured the strategic town of Kyindwe, [Kanpetlet Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanpetlet_Township) with the aid of the [Arakan Army](38106209.md) during the conclusion of the four-month long [Battle of Kyindwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Kyindwe).[^cite_note-469] The CC would then attempt to take [Tonzang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonzang) from the junta and its ally the [Zomi Revolutionary Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zomi_Revolutionary_Army)[^cite_note-470] capturing the town and neighbouring [Cikha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cikha) by 21 May.[^cite_note-471]&#x20;

The following week, the CBA moved on neighbouring [Tedim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tedim),[^cite_note-472] By mid-June, the junta pushing back from Tedim, recaptured positions along the Tedim-[Kalay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalay) road and took back Kennedy Peak.[^cite_note-473] Junta forces pushed as far as to recapture the strategic Tainngen village[^cite_note-474] before losing both Kennedy Peak and Taingen to [Chinland Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinland_Council)-led counteroffensives and retreating to Khaing Kham by 27 June.[^cite_note-475] On 27 July, the CNA and ZRA met in [Aizawl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aizawl) for peace negotiations.[^cite_note-476]&#x20;

On 30 May, CDF-Matupi captured the district administrative office on the Matupi-[Hakha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakha) road in [Matupi Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matupi_Township).[^cite_note-Matupi-81] On 24 June, CDFs clashed with junta forces outside of the Chin State capital [Hakha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakha). The junta retaliated by setting fires to homes in the town.[^cite_note-477] On 20 July, as part of "Operation Rung", the CDF-Hakha captured the Hakha Main Police Station, releasing 62 detainees.[^cite_note-478]&#x20;

##### *Operation Chin Brotherhood*



Matupi town, captured by Operation Chin Brotherhood on 29 June 2024

On 9 June, the CBA, Arakan Army and [Magway](493360.md)-based Yaw Army began "Operation Chin Brotherhood" attacking [Matupi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matupi,_Myanmar).[^cite_note-Matupi-81] Shortly after the announcement, the [Chinland Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinland_Council) issued a statement asking the Arakan Army to refrain from military and administrative operations in [Chinland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinland).[^cite_note-479] This came days after Global Khumi Organisation urged the Arakha Army against committing [human rights violations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_violations) against the [Khumi Chin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khumi_Chin) people of [Paletwa Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paletwa_Township).[^cite_note-480]&#x20;

Operation Chin Brotherhood continued with CBA aligned forces capturing one of the two junta battalion bases outside Matupi on 17 June.[^cite_note-481] Clashes between the CBA and CNA broke out during the push towards the final base, leading to 2 CBA deaths.[^cite_note-482] The CNA/Chinland Council was forced to retreat from the Matupi area by 24 June[^cite_note-483] On 29 June, the CBA captured the remaining junta base near Matupi and took over the town.[^cite_note-484]&#x20;

Infighting continued with CNA detaining other Yaw Defense Force soldiers[^cite_note-485] and MDF and AA taking Lailenpi, [Matupi Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matupi_Township) from the CDF-Mara and CNA.[^cite_note-486]&#x20;

#### Kachin theater

Main article: [2024 Kachin offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Kachin_offensive)

On 4 June renewed clashes broke out outside of [Momeik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Momeik).[^cite_note-487] A week later, KIA forces captured the Waingmaw-[Sadung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sadung)-[Kanpaikti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanpaikti) road, cutting off the state capital [Myitkyina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myitkyina) from the border.[^cite_note-488]&#x20;

On 15 July, junta forces launched an offensive to recapture areas near Momauk.[^cite_note-489] A month later, on 19 August, KIA forces captured the last junta base in Momauk, completing their capture of the township.[^cite_note-490] Shortly after, junta forces launched another counteroffensive to retake the town, but were stalled at Aung Myay village.[^cite_note-491]&#x20;

#### Karenni fighting

Main article: [Operation 1111 § Intensification in Loikaw and both on the offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1111#Intensification_in_Loikaw_and_both_on_the_offensive)

On 1 July, junta forces began attacking KNDF positions south of [Pinlaung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinlaung), attempting to relieve the Karenni siege on [Pekon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pekon).[^cite_note-492]&#x20;

In early July, fighting further intensified in Loikaw as Karenni resistance attempted to recapture areas of the town they had earlier been forced to withdraw from. The Irrawaddy reported on 8 July that fighting is intensifying after reinforcements of 500 troops had arrived to help the already stationed 1,500 Tatmadaw troops.[^cite_note-493]&#x20;

By mid July in [Hpasawng Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hpasawng_Township), there has been reports of fresh intense fighting, incl. Karenni claims of poisoned gas use by Tatmadaw against the resistance fighters.[^cite_note-494] Meanwhile, in Loikaw, pro-Tatmadaw sources confirm fighting in Loikaw, [Pekhon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pekon_clashes) and Mobye. In addition it claims Tatmadaw progressed in the direction of Loikaw University.[^cite_note-495]&#x20;

#### Battle of Lashio

Main article: [Battle of Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Lashio)



Map showing the [Battle of Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Lashio) during July–August 2024



Smoke rising from [Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashio) after the bombing, July 2024

On 2 July, the MNDAA began the [Battle of Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Lashio) against the heavily defended capital and largest town of northern [Shan State](493376.md). The TNLA joined the offensive the next day with the 2 armies attacking 4 junta bases surrounding Lashio. Pro-junta [Telegram](41501305.md) channels accused the [Shan State Progress Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shan_State_Progress_Party) (SSPP) of joining the offensive.[^cite_note-496] On 5 July, TNLA forces entered Lashio proper from the south.[^cite_note-497] The next day, the MNDAA captured Nampawng village south of Lashio, encircling the town, and began launching attacks into Lashio.[^cite_note-498]&#x20;

Concurrently MNDAA forces clashed with junta forces in [Mongyai Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongyai_Township)[^cite_note-499] and TNLA began launching attacks into [Momeik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Momeik).[^cite_note-500]&#x20;

Tensions and disputes rose between TNLA and the [Shan State Army (SSPP)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shan_State_Army_\(SSPP\)), with both sides asking the [United Wa State Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Wa_State_Army) (UWSA) for mediation.[^cite_note-501] On 10 July, the TNLA attacked junta positions near [Tangyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tangyan,_Myanmar).[^cite_note-502] Following an agreement with the Tatmadaw, the neutral UWSA deployed thousands of soldiers around Tangyan to prevent clashes from spreading to the area.[^cite_note-503] On 13 July, SSPP forces occupied [Mongyai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongyai), with residents claiming that clashes might break out in the town due to the apparent SSPP-junta agreement.[^cite_note-504]&#x20;

On 25 July, in a "historic victory", the MNDAA claimed to have captured the [Northeastern Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeastern_Regional_Military_Command_\(Myanmar\)) headquarters of Lashio, the first regional command headquarters to ever be captured by resistance forces.[^cite_note-505] On 30 July, MNDAA forces ambushed junta reinforcements attempting to reach Lashio, inflicting heavy casualties.[^cite_note-506] On 3 August, the capture of Lashio and the Northeastern Command headquarters was confirmed, with hundreds of Burmese soldiers surrendering.[^cite_note-Lashio-84] The Northeastern Command had exiled their leadership to [Muse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muse,_Myanmar), which remained the last junta stronghold in the area due to its proximity with the Chinese border.[^cite_note-507]&#x20;

#### Ceasefire attempts and "Shan-Man"

On 14 July, the MNDAA instituted a tenuous 4-day ceasefire after meeting with the [Chinese Communist Party](7175.md). Despite the ceasefire, clashes continued.[^cite_note-508][^cite_note-509] TNLA and SSPP leaders also met in [Panghsang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panghsang), Wa State, to discuss an end to the clashes. On 16 July, TNLA captured most of [Mongmit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongmit) and began launching attacks on the junta headquarters south of the town.[^cite_note-Mongmit-83]&#x20;

At the same time as the renewed 1027 offensive, the [NUG](67413816.md) announced that it had joined in the offensive through its [People's Defense Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Defense_Force_\(Myanmar\)) in [Mandalay Region](2357817.md), calling the attacks a "Shan Man" operation.[^cite_note-510] As part of Operation Shan-Man, the Mandalay People's Defence Force (MDY-PDF) captured 36 junta positions in late June and early July throughout the northern Mandalay Region.[^cite_note-511] The MDY-PDF and TNLA also captured Nawnghkio on 10 July[^cite_note-irrmajorgains-444] and [Singu Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singu_Township) by 12 July, further cutting off junta forces in Northern Shan State.[^cite_note-512]&#x20;



Mogok, Mandalay Region

On 20 July, the 3BA, reportedly under pressure from China, agreed to extend the ceasefire to 31 July.[^cite_note-513] Despite the agreements, junta reinforcements launched renewed attacks on Kyaukme on the same day.[^cite_note-514] On 24 July, TNLA and PDF forces completely captured [Mogok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mogok), Mandalay Region.[^cite_note-515]&#x20;

The TNLA captured the Shan state towns of [Momeik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Momeik) on 31 July[^cite_note-516] and [Kyaukme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukme,_Shan_State) on 6 August.[^cite_note-517] On 10 August, TNLA forces launched an offensive against [Hsipaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hsipaw), capturing the town's prison[^cite_note-518] and then nearby Nawngkawgyi village by 13 August.[^cite_note-519]&#x20;

On 12 August, MDY-PDF captured [Tagaung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tagaung), the first historical capital of the [Burmese monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tagaung_Kingdom), after a one-day battle.[^cite_note-520] The following week, Mandalay-PDF captured [Thabeikkyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thabeikkyin), capturing the town and its surroundings. The entirety of [Thabeikkyin District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thabeikkyin_District) came under rebel control.[^cite_note-Thabeikkyin-521]&#x20;

### Stalemates, Chinese pressure, and western front escalations (August 2024 – March 2025)

After the fall of Lashio, the junta launched counter offensives, but facing an inability to make progress, changed its tactics to favour [aerial bombardment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerial_bombardment) to destroy towns they could not take, like Lashio.[^cite_note-522] The rebels, too, have failed to make as much progress due to diplomatic pressures and supply issues. Analyst Yun Sun from the [Stimson Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stimson_Center) noted Director of the [Office of the Central Foreign Affairs Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_the_Central_Foreign_Affairs_Commission) [Wang Yi](18211536.md)'s visit in mid-August as indicative of a shift in China's stance from greenlighting Operation 1027 in November 2023 to endorsing the [State Administration Council](66589972.md) and criticising the [Northern Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Alliance_\(Myanmar\)) and the [United Wa State Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Wa_State_Army) (UWSA) for not standing down.[^cite_note-523] There are signs that China is accelerating weapons transfers to the junta and restricting key ammunition supplies to the TNLA, affecting the resistance's realistic prospects of taking [Mandalay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandalay) despite the junta being in its weakest position since 2021.[^cite_note-524] However, this recent alignment with the military regime has strained its influence over border groups like the 3BA, diminishing China's sway and ability to mediate in this new landscape.[^cite_note-525] China's support temporarily slowed the rebels' momentum but failed to reverse the trend of humiliating losses, including the fall of a second Regional Military Command in [Ann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ann,_Myanmar), Rakhine State. Instead, China finds its interests threatened as the Arakan Army surround [Kyaukphyu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukphyu) and Kachin groups seize control of rare earth mineral mines.[^cite_note-ymh-2025-446] Chinese-backed peace talks and pressure for MNDAA to retreat from [Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashio) continued in early 2025 but failed in late March 2025.[^cite_note-marchfailcf-526]&#x20;

In January 2025, *The New York Times* reported that the rebels were opening colleges and universities in the territory they controlled.[^cite_note-527]&#x20;

#### Operation Hsinphyushin



Street in Nawnghkio

In late August 2024, the Tatmadaw began preparing an offensive to recapture key towns in northern Shan State, terming it as "Operation Hsin Phyu Shin" (after the Burmese king [Hsinbyushin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hsinbyushin)).[^cite_note-528] Throughout September and October, the Tatmadaw began massing forces in the final towns it controlled in Northern Shan State (Namlan, Mongyai, and Tangyan) in preparation for the counteroffensive to retake Lashio.[^cite_note-529]&#x20;

This came in the context of strained China-3BA relations. On 29 August, the [Ruili City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruili_City) Security Commission issued a statement demanding the TNLA to end all offensive operations against the junta, threatening "more deterrent and disciplinary measures" if it did not.[^cite_note-530] 4 days later, the junta designated the [Three Brotherhood Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Brotherhood_Alliance) and its members as "terrorist groups".[^cite_note-531] On 19 September, the MNDAA announced that it would end its offensive operations, further stating it would refuse to ever work with the [National Unity Government of Myanmar](67413816.md).[^cite_note-532] Despite the Chinese threat, the TNLA captured the last junta base in Hsipaw on 13 October.[^cite_note-533]&#x20;

On 19 October, Tatmadaw forces launched a counteroffensive, aimed at retaking Nawnghkio instead of Lashio.[^cite_note-534]&#x20;

On 18 November, it was confirmed that MNDAA leader [Peng Daxun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peng_Daxun) had been detained in [Yunnan](166410.md) by Chinese authorities since late October, further pressuring MNDAA forces to relinquish Lashio to the junta.[^cite_note-535]&#x20;

In early December, the MNDAA declared a unilateral ceasefire and expressed willingness to engage in peace talks with the junta, with China acting as a mediator.[^cite_note-536] The MNDAA announced its readiness to send a high-level delegation for dialogue to resolve conflicts through political means.[^cite_note-537] However, despite this announcement, airstrikes on TNLA-held areas continued.[^cite_note-538] On 20 January 2025, the MNDAA and the junta agreed to a Chinese-brokered ceasefire, with several sources claiming that a withdraw of the former from Lashio was a condition for the deal. However, at the time neither the junta nor the MNDAA revealed any details of the deal.[^cite_note-539] The pressure for MNDAA to retreat from [Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashio) continued but ceasefire talks failed in late March 2025.[^cite_note-marchfailcf-526]&#x20;

#### Renewed southern Shan assault

In early August, the PNLA launched a renewed assault on the outskirts of Hsi Hseng after junta forces in the town bombed the PNLA headquarters in [Mawkmai Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mawkmai_Township).[^cite_note-540] At the same time, the junta-allied PNO intensified military preparations by training 10,000 militia members in Pinlaung Township, in southern Shan State, aimed at bolstering the defense of Naypyitaw.[^cite_note-541] On 10 September, the PNLO split after disputes surrounding a ceasefire with the junta, with pro-ceasefire leaders breaking off to form the "PNLO-NCA-S."[^cite_note-542]&#x20;

On 19 September, the Tatmadaw and allied Lahu military attacked a [Shan State Army (RCSS)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shan_State_Army_\(RCSS\)) base in Yanghkam, [Mong Ping Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mong_Ping_Township).[^cite_note-543]&#x20;

#### Continued attacks in the Dry Zone

Main article: [Dry Zone theater § 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dry_Zone_theater#2024)



PDF forces in [Magway Region](493360.md), August 2024

The People's Defence Forces also continued fighting outside of the Shan-Man context. On 11 June, the Union Liberation Front and Sagaing Region People's Defence Organisation captured a junta base near the Sagaing capital [Monywa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monywa).[^cite_note-544] On 27 June, a coalition of several PDFs attacked [Budalin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budalin).[^cite_note-545]&#x20;

On 17 July, People's Defense Forces launched 2 rockets at [Nay Pyi Taw International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nay_Pyi_Taw_International_Airport), causing no casualties.[^cite_note-546]&#x20;

On 10 August, PDFs in [Myingyan District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myingyan_District), central Mandalay Region, launched an offensive against [Taungtha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taungtha,_Mandalay) and [Natogyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natogyi).[^cite_note-547] Natogyi was briefly captured before being recaptured by the junta soon after. On 15 August, PDF launched an offensive on [Tabayin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabayin), Sagaing Region, again capturing the town by 18 August briefly before withdrawing shortly after due to junta counterattacks.[^cite_note-548] In mid-August, the KIA and PDF launched joint offensives on [Indaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indaw), [Pinlebu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinlebu), and [Htigyaing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Htigyaing), Sagain Region.[^cite_note-549] Resistance forces captured Pinlebu on 8 October, defeating almost 800 junta soldiers.[^cite_note-550]&#x20;

On 11 November, the [Burma National Revolutionary Army](71438986.md) and several other PDF's launched attacks on [Pale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pale,_Myanmar) before being forced to retreat due to junta counterattacks.[^cite_note-551]&#x20;

#### Late 2024 Kachin offensives

Main article: [2024 Kachin offensive § Continued Fighting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Kachin_offensive#Continued_Fighting)

On 7 September, KIA forces began attacking Singtawn—outside Hpakant—after junta forces raided a hotel in the area, capturing the village one month later.[^cite_note-552] In late September, it was reported that junta forces were preparing an offensive to retake Sumprabum and Tiyangzug.[^cite_note-553]&#x20;

On 29 September, the KIA launched an offensive on [Chipwi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipwi) and [Hsawlaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hsawlaw), capturing both towns by 2 October. Shortly after, the KIA asked the [New Democratic Army – Kachinto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Democratic_Army_%E2%80%93_Kachin) defect, but were denied.[^cite_note-554] In response, the KIA launched an offensive on Kachin BGF battalions 1002 and 1003, capturing both by 22 October.[^cite_note-555][^cite_note-556]&#x20;



Pang War, Myanmar on a snowy day

The KIA then captured the NDA-K's headquarters in the town of [Pang War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pang_War) on 20 October with Kachin BGF leader Zahkung Ting Ying fled to China.[^cite_note-557] On 1 November, the KIA launched an attack on the final BGF 1001 bases at Nuzaungbaung and Phimaw, capturing both by the next day. Border Guard Forces fled to [Kangfang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kangfang) (Ganfai), which the KIA captured days later.[^cite_note-558] On 10 November, KIA forces completed the capture of Kachin Special Region 1 and all of the Kachin BGF territory when they took Lanse village.[^cite_note-559] On 20 November, despite Chinese pressure, the KIA captured [Kanpaikti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanpaikti), the final Kachin BGF base.[^cite_note-560] This victory rendered the NDA-K virtually defeated on all fronts, with most members either deserting or surrendering to the KIA. Some BGF members joined pro-government militias later in the conflict. All assets of the NDA-K were confiscated by the KIA, giving the rebel group a major boost in revenue. By capturing more of the China-Myanmar border, the KIA could continue selling jade in larger quantities, giving licenses to private Chinese mining companies to operate on their territory.[^cite_note-561][^cite_note-562]&#x20;

In December, the KIA and allies attacked Myanmar junta targets in [Bhamo](2150645.md) and Mansi townships in the [Battle of Bhamo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Bhamo_\(2024\)).[^cite_note-563]&#x20;

#### Chin theatre

Main article: [Chin theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_theatre)

In early August, the [Chin National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_National_Army) and allies took in [Thantlang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thantlang) after about a week of fighting.[^cite_note-564]&#x20;

On 9 November, the [Chin Brotherhood Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_Brotherhood_Alliance) relaunched Operation Chin Brotherhood, launching simultaneous offensives on [Falam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falam) and [Mindat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mindat,_Chin_State), capturing most of the former.[^cite_note-565]&#x20;

In December 2024, the Chin Brotherhood Alliance (CBA) achieved significant victories in Chin State. On 15 December 123 junta troops and police officers from Mindat district and township police compounds, together with their families, surrendered to the CBA.[^cite_note-566] On 21 December, the CBA declared Mindat a junta-free zone following the rescue of 13 political prisoners.[^cite_note-567] These victories further consolidated the CBA's control in the region and demonstrated the strength of Operation Chin Brotherhood.&#x20;

#### Advances in Rakhine and the fall of Ann

Main articles: [Battle of Ann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Ann) and [Battle of Maungdaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Maungdaw)



On 20 December 2024, the Arakan Army (AA) claimed to have captured the Myanmar military's [Western Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Regional_Military_Command_\(Myanmar\)) headquarters in Ann, Rakhine State.

After the capture of Thandwe, [Arakan Army](38106209.md) forces launched an offensive south towards [Gwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwa,_Myanmar), capturing [Kyeintali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyeintali) by 14 August 2024.[^cite_note-568] On 5 September, the AA captured Maung Shwe Lay [Naval Base](9502915.md), the first naval base to be captured by resistance forces.[^cite_note-569] On 25 September, the AA launched an offensive on the remaining bases in Ann. By 25 October, the AA captured the remaining Mae Taung bases, encircled Ann, and captured the town's [airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ann_Airport).[^cite_note-570] On 7 November, the AA launched an offensive on neighboring [Taungup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taungup).[^cite_note-571] The Arakan Army captured Maungdaw in the first half of December, after securing the junta's last remaining position there. The AA captured General Thurein Tun during the operation.[^cite_note-572][^cite_note-573]&#x20;



[Kyauktaw Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyauktaw_Township) bombed by [Myanmar Air Force](9484818.md), January 2025

In December 2024, the AA achieved significant victories in Rakhine State. On 8 December, the AA captured Maungdaw Township, securing full control over the 271-kilometer border with Bangladesh.[^cite_note-574] This strategic gain was followed by the seizure of the [Western Regional Command headquarters](9503548.md#Bureau_of_Special_Operations_\(BSO\)) in Ann Township on 20 December, marking the second regional command center to fall to ethnic armed groups within five months.[^cite_note-575] On 29 December, the AA extended its campaign by seizing full control of Gwa Township, the southernmost town in Rakhine State, after junta troops retreated from the area.[^cite_note-576] This victory further consolidated the AA's dominance in the region, leaving only a few townships under junta control.[^cite_note-577] The AA's leadership has since expressed readiness to engage in political dialogue to seek a resolution to the ongoing conflict.[^cite_note-578]&#x20;

In January 2025, Arakan Army and joint PDF forces pushed into [Shwethaungyan Subtownship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shwethaungyan_Subtownship), northwest [Ayeyarwaddy Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayeyarwaddy_Region) taking the village of Magyizin.[^cite_note-579] Battles with the junta intensified in the area as the [Myanmar Navy](9502915.md) increased bombardments to the Bawmi coastline area near Magyizin by 20 January 2025.[^cite_note-580] By late March, AA had reached the eastern side of the Arakan mountains taking villages in [Lemyethna Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lemyethna_Township) near the Pathein-Monywa Highway.[^cite_note-581]&#x20;

#### Karen State clashes and key KNLA advances

After the Karen National Army set a five-month deadline in May 2024 for Chinese scamming operatives to leave Myawaddy, many have migrated to the [Three Pagodas Pass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Pagodas_Pass), controlled by the [Democratic Karen Benevolent Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Karen_Benevolent_Army).[^cite_note-582] Throughout May, the KNLA, aided by allies, captured all but three of the remaining junta bases in [Hpapun Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hpapun_Township).[^cite_note-583]&#x20;

On 1 June, clashes erupted between KNLA-led forces and joint Karen National Army/junta forces near Tonetatdar. A source close to the junta claimed that [Karen National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_National_Army) (KNA) soldiers were coordinating to join with junta soldiers from Operation Aung Zeya to retake Myawaddy, Thingannyinaung and to attack the [Asian Highway 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Highway_1) (AH1).[^cite_note-584] However, after being stalled for months by ambushes on the AH1, the Aung Zeya column was forced to retreat to [Kawkareik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawkareik).[^cite_note-585]&#x20;

On 9 August, KNLA forces clashed with junta reinforcements near Zayatkyi, [Htantabin Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Htantabin_Township).[^cite_note-586]&#x20;

In December 2024, the KNLA achieved significant victories in Kayin State. On 17 December, the KNLA attacked the Swe Taw Kone junta camp near Lay Kay Kaw village, capturing prisoners and seizing weapons.[^cite_note-587] Additionally, the KNLA recaptured its former headquarters, Manerplaw, on the Thai border, further consolidating its control in the region.[^cite_note-588]&#x20;

#### Shifting Karenni frontlines

Main article: [Operation 1111 § Fighting along the Karenni-Shan Border area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1111#Fighting_along_the_Karenni-Shan_Border_area)

In early August, the Tatmadaw and civilian sources reported that the Tatmadaw had secured [Loikaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loikaw) and much of [the surrounding township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loikaw_Township).[^cite_note-589] On 5 September, the KNDF attempted to assassinate Tatmadaw leader [Min Aung Hlaing](38278640.md) while he was visiting Loikaw.[^cite_note-590]&#x20;

As of 20 September 2024, the Myanmar junta only controlled Loikaw and Bawlakhe townships.[^cite_note-591]&#x20;

During October, fighting shifted to North Kayah State after the flooding of [Typhoon Yagi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhoon_Yagi). Tatmadaw troops and Pa-O militia attacked several Karenni resistance-held settlements, including Pinlaung, Mobye and Pekon. Pekon was taken in a military counter-offensive, taking advantage of the flooding caused by Yagi.[^cite_note-592] However, Karenni leadership did not believe that the Tatmadaw aimed to hold the territory, as the Tatmadaw needed helicopters to maintain some supply lines.[^cite_note-593][^cite_note-594] Fighting was reported in early December along the Pekon-Moebye-Loikaw road.[^cite_note-595]&#x20;

On 29 October 2024, several Karenni resistance groups were reported to have united as the [Kayan National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayan_National_Army).[^cite_note-596]&#x20;

In January 2025, the town of [Demoso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demoso) was reported to have become a major centre for refugees, housing some 150,000 displaced civilians.[^cite_note-597]&#x20;

##### *Fighting over Mobye*

Main article: [Operation 1111 § Fight for control of Mobye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1111#Fight_for_control_of_Mobye)

Tatmadaw has succeeded in taking Mobye, the town, during its offensive in December. Now as of early February the KNDF launches an advance to retake the town.[^cite_note-KR-598]&#x20;

In early March, there were reports of air and artillery strikes in Pekhon and Loikaw. While KNDF had announced it had launched an offensive to retake Mobye from Tatmadaw, who had taken it in December.[^cite_note-KR-598] The junta was reported to have taken high casualties when taking the town. With KNDF now defending against a junta bid to "reclaim" Mobye from KNDF. The junta sent reinforcements from Loikaw to help in this effort. Additional areas are mentioned, with the Irrawaddy citing an KNDF member saying: "Fighting has also broken out in \[Karenni’s] Loikaw, Hpasaung, and Bawlakhae townships over the past month, but Mobye has seen the heaviest clashes. The junta has suffered significant casualties while we have also lost some comrades". Mobye is strategically located 40 km from Loikaw.[^cite_note-599]&#x20;

### Earthquake and aftermath (March 2025 – present)



Junta leader Min Aung Hlaing gains international recognition after the earthquake at the BIMSTEC Summit

On 28 March 2025, the [2025 Myanmar earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Myanmar_earthquake) struck, deepening the nation's humanitarian crisis and shifting the focus of the civil war. The resistance, junta, and EAOs all declared various unilateral ceasefires that were observed to varying degrees.[^cite_note-600] The junta continued airstrikes on civilian targets under the guise of defending against the resistance. It used the chaos to position itself as the only actor capable of coordinating a disaster response, attempting to block aid efforts of the resistance to reinforce its authority.[^cite_note-601] But this also gave the resistance a chance to show its state-building efforts to gain legitimacy as a governing body in resistance-held areas by organising aid and evacuation and highlighting the junta's attempts to block relief.[^cite_note-irrearthquake-602] Insurgent dynamics continued to evolve with [Arakan Army](38106209.md) expanding its influence through a loose network of allied resistance groups, projecting its power out of Rakhine State into neighbouring regions.[^cite_note-603]&#x20;

The earthquake also entangled the civil war with international actors as relief organisations navigated the complex situation on the ground.[^cite_note-irrearthquake-602] Far from being a moment of unity, the disaster allowed external actors to consolidate influence and the military to secure its symbolic authority rather than delivering relief equitably. Beyond aid, [China](5405.md) orchestrated the MNDAA's handover of [Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashio) to the junta in part by exploiting the post-earthquake political vacuum to tighten its grip on Myanmar's borderlands.[^cite_note-604] Analysts describe Beijing' involvement as part of a broader strategy to influence and create stability along its border through its leverage over both sides of the conflict.[^cite_note-605] Meanwhile, offensives continued in areas unaffected by the earthquake or ceasefire declarations, like in [Indaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indaw) and [Karen State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_State).&#x20;

The Tatmadaw proceeded with [long-delayed general elections](69631013.md) across three phases in areas under their control through December 2025 and January 2026. Major opposition parties were barred from participating and regions affected by conflict were unable to vote.[^cite_note-606] Analysts argued the vote was held to legitimise continued military rule rather than resolve the conflict.[^cite_note-607] Observers noted that the Tatmadaw did little to reduce hostilities during the election, continuing airstrikes in regions holding voting and killing at least 170.[^cite_note-608][^cite_note-609] Resistance groups also targeted individuals and places associated with the election.[^cite_note-610] The [election in Loikaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1111#2025_Elections_in_Loikaw) was reported to be restless, with forced voting and bombings.[^cite_note-611]&#x20;

#### Declared and broken ceasefires



Damage in [Mandalay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandalay) from the 2025 Myanmar earthquake

After the earthquake, the NUG announced a two week unilateral pause in offensive actions to coordinate humanitarian efforts with the UN and non-governmental groups and provide access to resistance held areas.[^cite_note-quakecf-86]&#x20;

The air base in [Monywa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monywa) continued operations against resistance-held [Chaung-U Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaung-U_Township), deploying a paradrop attack hours after the earthquake.[^cite_note-612] The following day, the junta resumed aerial bombardment on territories held by resistance forces in Karen, northern Shan, Bago and Sagaing regions.[^cite_note-613] The [People's Defence Force](67819275.md), a rebel force, said they would observe a partial [ceasefire](592591.md) for two weeks beginning on 30 March.[^cite_note-614] Despite this, the junta conducted a bombing campaign in [Pauk Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pauk_Township), Sagaing Region.[^cite_note-615] An airstrike in [Singu Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singu_Township), Mandalay, on 31 March injured several residents and burnt down their homes.[^cite_note-616]&#x20;

On 1 April 2025, the [Three Brotherhood Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Brotherhood_Alliance) announced a one-month unilateral humanitarian pause, refraining from offensive operations in conflict areas to facilitate post-earthquake rescue efforts.[^cite_note-617] But junta leader [Min Aung Hlaing](38278640.md) announced on [Myawaddy TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myawaddy_TV) that he would continue military operations against rebels.[^cite_note-618] The next day, the military fired warning shots at a [Chinese Red Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Cross_Society_of_China) aid convoy.[^cite_note-619] Later that day, Min Aung Hlaing announced that he would implement a 20-day ceasefire until 22 April.[^cite_note-620] The KIA announced that it would similarly cease offensive operations later that day.[^cite_note-621] The next day, the junta conducted an aerial bombing campaign in [Indaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indaw), Sagaing Region and continued firing artillery in [Bhamo](2150645.md), Kachin State.[^cite_note-20_day_ceasefire-88] By 7 April, joint ABSDF and PDF forces had captured Indaw completely.[^cite_note-622]&#x20;

On 1 May, The Three Brotherhood Alliance again announced a ceasefire, extending it to 31 May for the extended support of post-earthquake recovery.[^cite_note-623] Despite ceasefires, fighting in [Nawnghkio Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nawnghkio_Township) continued, with the TNLA losing eight outposts to the junta.[^cite_note-624] The junta also continued bombings to push a counteroffensive to retake [Madaya Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madaya_Township) in Mandalay Region.[^cite_note-625]&#x20;

On 12 May, the junta [launched airstrikes on a village school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Depayin_School_Bombing) in [Depayin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabayin), killing around 50, mostly children, despite the declared ceasefire.[^cite_note-626]&#x20;

#### Junta counteroffensives in Mandalay and Shan

In July, the junta successfully pushed back against the TNLA and allied PDF forces, recapturing [Nawnghkio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nawnghkio) in northeast Shan State on 15 July.[^cite_note-auto-627] A junta flotilla aided troops to land in [Thabeikkyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thabeikkyin) in northern Mandalay Region, capturing the town on 23 July.[^cite_note-628] As part of this amphibious operation, the junta also ramped up airstrikes in southern Sagain to protect the flotilla.[^cite_note-auto-627] However, the flotilla was ambushed further upstream after Thabeikkyin north of [Shwegu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shwegu) by PDF forces, preventing it from aiding in the continuing [Battle of Bhamo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Bhamo_\(2024\)).[^cite_note-629] As of November 2025, the battle is still ongoing.[^cite_note-630] As of 30th November, Tatmadaw has begun an counter-offensive to retake all of the city.[^cite_note-631]&#x20;

##### *Withdrawal from Lashio*

On 18 April, junta administrators and the [Myanmar Police Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myanmar_Police_Force) re-entered [Lashio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lashio) as part of a deal with the MNDAA. Reportedly, the junta will control the city; the MNDAA retains administration over Lashio's outskirts.[^cite_note-632] Over the following two days, a convoy of vehicles flying the Chinese flag, whose occupants identified themselves as members of the Ceasefire Monitoring Group, drove into Lashio from [China](5405.md). On 21 April, the MNDAA lowered its flags in Lashio but its police officers and administrators functioned as before. The MNDAA then retreated to checkpoints five miles north and three miles south, continuing to surround the city and control portions of Lashio's outer wards.[^cite_note-lashiowithdraw-93]&#x20;

##### *Tatmadaw's defeat of TNLA*

Main article: [Battle for Mogok](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Battle_for_Mogok\&action=edit\&redlink=1)



Road to Mogok

In early October 2025, the junta recaptured the district-level town of [Kyaukme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukme) from the TNLA as part of their ongoing counteroffensive.[^cite_note-kyaukmeretaken-91] By mid-October, Hsipaw fell to the Junta.[^cite_note-633] On 29 October, the TNLA signed a ceasefire with the Tatmadaw and agreed to cede [Mogok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mogok) and [Mongmit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongmit).[^cite_note-634] TNLA Major General Tar Bone Kyaw stated that they were forced to sign the agreement because of ammunition and funding shortages.[^cite_note-tnlawithdraw-92]&#x20;

After the TNLA withdrawal, the PDF moved into Mogok to take control of it.[^cite_note-635] The Tatmadaw continued to advance against the PDF, taking among other places the Sedawgyi Dam.[^cite_note-636] By mid-November, BNI reported that the Tatmadaw counter-offensive had retaken numerous cities amounting to 11.3% of the land it lost during [Operation 1027](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1027).[^cite_note-637] The Ta'ang National Army, as of mid-November, threatened the PDF that it would 'escort' the Tatmadaw into Mogok.[^cite_note-638] In the second half of November, accusations were levied against TNLA over the cession of Mogok.[^cite_note-639] Amid pressure, the PDF gave up Mogok.[^cite_note-640]&#x20;

Fighting broke out between the Tatmadaw and KIA along the Mogok–[Momeik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Momeik) road. The PDF was reported to be fighting alongside KIA.[^cite_note-641] Amid the fighting, Tatmadaw opted to set fire to parts of Mogok.[^cite_note-642] Fighting for the town of Mogok itself continued into December.[^cite_note-643] The TNLA then deployed troops to protect Tatmadaw in Mongmit and along the Mongmit-Mantong road, preventing an advance by the rebels from the east. The Tatmadaw dug defensive trenches in Mongmit to prevent KIA and PDF from taking the town.[^cite_note-644]&#x20;

In December, the Tatmadaw also took control of [Singu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singu) in northern [Mandalay Region](2357817.md), using a [pincer movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pincer_movement) to retake the towns connecting Mandalay to Mogok.[^cite_note-645]&#x20;

By mid January 2026, junta took control of Mogok and Momeik with the PDF present in the cities before TNLA's handover leaving for nearby positions. Fighting between the two continued in January under the newly established Mogok-Momeik Region Operation Command of the NUG.[^cite_note-646] by 23 January, the Tatmadaw control solidified enough for the Mogok-Mandalay road to be reopened to the public.[^cite_note-647]&#x20;

In mid-March, the alliance between MNDAA and TNLA broke down over the issue of security cameras and to reopen the trade route to China. The MNDAA attacked TNLA-controlled [Kutkai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kutkai) using drones and took the city on 16 March.[^cite_note-648][^cite_note-649] PDF exited Mogok, surrendering it to Tatmadaw.[^cite_note-650]&#x20;

##### *Sagaing-Kachin front*

The PDF and allied KIA forces captured several Tatmadaw and [Shanni Nationalities Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanni_Nationalities_Army) bases near the town of [Banmauk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banmauk) starting on 17 September 2025 beginning the [Battle of Banmauk](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Battle_of_Banmauk\&action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-banmauk-651] Banmauk was then besieged by the Shanni National Army.[^cite_note-banmauk-651] However, three days later, the PDF and the KIA still captured Banmauk in late September.[^cite_note-652]&#x20;

Fighting in Banmauk picked up in December.[^cite_note-653] By January 2026, the junta recaptured half of Banmauk,[^cite_note-654] and by February 8, most of the town had fallen to the SNA and Tatmadaw.[^cite_note-655]&#x20;

The PDF continued to contest [Katha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katha,_Myanmar), entering the town in December 2025.[^cite_note-656]&#x20;

#### Continued offensives outside earthquake areas



Road near Falam in 2016

In Chin State, the district capital [Falam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falam) was captured by the [Chin Brotherhood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chin_Brotherhood) on 7 April 2025.[^cite_note-falam-657]&#x20;

On 14 April 2025, a joint force of the [Karen National Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_National_Liberation_Army) and the PDF launched a coordinated offensive against junta’s headquarters and bases in [Kyondoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyondoe), southern [Karen State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_State).[^cite_note-658] On 9 May, the KNLA and its allies and seized the junta’s [Htee Khee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Htee_Khee) base on the Thai border in [Dawei Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dawei_Township), [Tanintharyi Region](15162240.md), capturing the border trade hub and strategic link in the oil and gas trade route between Dawei and Thailand.[^cite_note-659] In November, The KNLA also captured Mawdaung in the [Singkhon Pass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singkhon_Pass) along the Thai border.[^cite_note-660]&#x20;

On 10 June, the armed forces of the Burmese Communist Party shot down a junta fighter jet during the battle for Kan Dauk police station in Pale Township, Sagaing Region. It was the tenth such shoot-down since 2021.[^cite_note-661] By 19 June, the Communists had taken the village and pushed out the junta forces.[^cite_note-662]&#x20;

##### *Area around Myawaddy*

Main article: [Battle of Myawaddy (2025)](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Battle_of_Myawaddy_\(2025\)\&action=edit\&redlink=1)

In September 2025, the junta renewed efforts to control [Myawaddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myawaddy) in Karen State. They began their offensive by advancing some 14 kilometres against the KNLA.[^cite_note-663] By October, [Lay Kay Kaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lay_Kay_Kaw), south of the city, was stormed by the Tatmadaw.[^cite_note-664] Fierce fighting nearby continued during late October with Tatmadaw making gains and Lay Kay Kaw changing hands.[^cite_note-665]&#x20;

As of January 2026, there was fresh fighting in Myawaddy. The [Karen National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_National_Army), which completed their process to become an independent group in the beginning of 2026, retreated from the area.[^cite_note-666][^cite_note-667][^cite_note-668]&#x20;

##### *Tatmadaw offensive in Rakhine*

Main article: [Rakhine offensive (2023–present) § Tatmadaw's counter offensive](76441089.md#Tatmadaw's_counter_offensive)

The junta also continued bombing campaigns in [Rakhine State](493373.md), destroying parts of the hospital in [Arakan Army](38106209.md)-controlled [Ponnagyun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ponnagyun) on 28 March, with the Arakan Army continuing [offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rakhine_offensive_\(2023-present\)) operations in [Kyaukphyu Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukphyu_Township).[^cite_note-669][^cite_note-670] Through the rest of 2025, the Arakan Army also had several clashes with the Tatmadaw.[^cite_note-671]&#x20;

In September 2025, the Tatmadaw launched an offensive to relieve encircled cities in Rahkine, with some initial victories for the Arakan Army.[^cite_note-672][^cite_note-673] As heavy fighting continued,[^cite_note-674] more Arakan victories were reported.[^cite_note-675] The Tatmadaw sent reinforcements to the Sittwe front in October[^cite_note-676] and advanced their position against the AA near [Kyaukphyu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyaukphyu) in late October.[^cite_note-677] In November, the [Battle of Kyaukphyu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Kyaukphyu) began over control of the township.[^cite_note-678] Fighting raged between the Tatmadaw and Arakan Army, with some territorial advances reported for the latter.[^cite_note-679]&#x20;

The [Battle of Sittwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Sittwe) regained intensity in January 2026 as the AA seized key outposts.[^cite_note-680] The Tatmadaw intensified efforts to enter Arakan territory from the south in February.[^cite_note-681] However, in early March, Arakan Army advanced to within 1 mile of the capital.[^cite_note-682]&#x20;

##### *Kayah State*

Main articles: [Operation 1111 § Earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1111#Earthquake), and [Operation 1111 § Battles along roads north continues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1111#Battles_along_roads_north_continues)

The quake affected residents, limiting their water access. However, combat also continued in the Kayah theatre with junta airstrikes in Hrupso and Loikaw townships. Reportedly, some Karenni civilians initially confused the earthquake with airstrikes.[^cite_note-683][^cite_note-684]&#x20;

Several villages saw combat along the Loikaw-Hsihseng road.[^cite_note-685] An undercover [BBC](19344654.md) team reported daily fighting in Mobye, with frontlines some 100 meters from a military base located there.[^cite_note-686] In late June, fighting continued to be intense with homes in [Saung Nan Khae](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Saung_Nan_Khae\&action=edit\&redlink=1) being burnt by the junta.[^cite_note-687][^cite_note-688] On 3 July, the Tatmadaw took [Mobye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobye) back.[^cite_note-689]&#x20;

The village of [Bawyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bawyan) saw heavy fighting starting in September 2025.[^cite_note-690] The Tatmadaw's armoured columns moved into Bawyan and its neighbour two villages of Salong North and Salong South. In early December, the Karenni coalition launched a counter-offensive in [Pekon Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pekon_Township), around the village of Salong. Both sides suffered casualties amidst intense fighting near Bawyan and Salong involving artillery and drones.[^cite_note-691]&#x20;

On 19 August 2025, the Tatmadaw claimed control of [Demoso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demoso) in the [Battle for Demoso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1111#Demoso).[^cite_note-auto3-692] Karenni resistance forces denied the junta had taken full control of the town.[^cite_note-auto1-693] By end of August, fighting was still ongoing, with control of the town split between Tatmadaw and Karenni resistance.[^cite_note-694] Despite claims of fighting by the Karenni resistance, by October, Demoso was reportedly fully in Tatmadaw hands.[^cite_note-695][^cite_note-auto1-693][^cite_note-auto3-692][^cite_note-auto2-696]&#x20;

In February 2026, the Tatmadaw was reported to have broken the [siege of Hpasawng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_1111#Siege_of_Hpasawng) with "overwhelming" forces, employing artillery and suicide drones to take the town back from the KNDF. The KNDF restricted access to the Thanlwin River located three kilometres south of the town. The Tatmadaw reportedly fought dozens of clashes with the Karenni resistance.[^cite_note-697]&#x20;

Fighting in the vicinity of Loikaw and elsewhere has seen skirmishes occur in September 2026, with casualties reported.[^cite_note-698][^cite_note-699]&#x20;

#### Formation of new alliances

In December 2025, the [Spring Revolution Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spring_Revolution_Alliance) was formed by 19 member groups largely consisting of groups adjacent to the PDF, forming a 10,000 member coalition.[^cite_note-sra-6][^cite_note-700]&#x20;

In January 2026, a new [Chin People's Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chin_People%27s_Army\&action=edit\&redlink=1) was formed by merging CDF–Mindat, CDF–Matupi, CDF–Kanpetlet, and CDF–Daai.[^cite_note-701]&#x20;
